from their counterparts
f^{\ast}=(B,B^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\otimes_{(A,A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(-)\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X):=\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A,A^{+})\to\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(Y):=\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B,B^{+})
, we denote them by
\hat{f}^{\ast},\hat{f}_{\ast}
.

(c)

We let
\alpha_{X}^{\ast}\colon\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X)=\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{\circ},A^{+}))\to\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X)
be the functor induced on module categories by the symmetric monoidal functor
\widehat{(A^{\circ},A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}\otimes_{(A^{\circ},A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(-)
from
Definition

2.4
. We let
\alpha_{X,\ast}
be the right adjoint of
\alpha_{X}^{\ast}
.

Let
\mathrm{Sym}
be the
\infty
-category of cocomplete closed symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-categories with morphisms given by cocontinuous, symmetric monoidal functors, cf.
[
7
, Definition 6.3]
.
We recall from
[
7
, Proposition 6.5]
that a morphism
g^{\ast}\colon C\to D
in
\mathrm{Sym}
is called an open (resp. closed) immersion if
g^{\ast}
has a fully faithful left adjoint
g_{!}
(resp. colimit preserving fully faithful right adjoint
g_{\ast}
), which satisfies the projection formula. If
g^{\ast}
is a closed immersion, then
g_{\ast}(1_{D})\in C
is an idempotent algebra, and
D\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{g_{\ast}(1_{D})}(C)
. If
g^{\ast}
is an open immersion, then the cofiber
R:=[g_{!}(1_{D})\to 1_{C}]\in C
is an idempotent algebra, and
D
is isomorphic to the quotient
C/\mathrm{Mod}_{R}(C)
(via
g^{\ast}
). We record the following stability of open/closed immersions under base change with respect to the Lurie tensor product.

Lemma 2.35
.

Let
h^{\ast}\colon C\to C^{\prime}
,
g^{\ast}\colon C\to D
be morphisms in
\mathrm{Sym}
. If
g^{\ast}
is an open (resp. closed) immersion, the same is true for the base change
k^{\ast}\colon C^{\prime}\to D^{\prime}:=C^{\prime}\otimes_{C}D
.

Proof.

For closed immersions this is clear as
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}(C)\otimes_{C}C^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{h^{\ast}(R)}(C^{\prime})
for any monoid object
R\in C
, and
h^{\ast}
preserves idempotent algebras. Thus, assume that
g^{\ast}
is an open immersion, and let
R:=[g_{!}(1_{D})\to 1_{C}]\in C
be the associated idempotent algebra. As the base change
C^{\prime}\otimes_{C}(-)
preserves adjunctions between colimit preserving,
C
-linear functors, the base change
k_{!}
of
g_{!}
is a fully left adjoint of
k^{\ast}
. By
C^{\prime}
-linearity one checks that
k_{!}
satisfies the projection formula. In particular,
k^{\ast}
is an open immersion. Moreover, we note that as in the proof of
[
7
, Proposition 6.5]
the projection formula for
k^{\ast}
implies that the kernel of the functor
k^{\ast}
is exactly the category of
h^{\ast}(R)
-modules in
C^{\prime}
because
h^{\ast}(R)
is the cofiber of
k_{!}(1_{D^{\prime}})\to 1_{C^{\prime}}
by exactness of
h^{\ast}
.
∎

Remark 2.36
.

The last part of the proof of
Lemma

2.35
shows that base change preserves the “excision sequence”
D\mathbin{\xrightarrow{g_{!}}}C\to\mathrm{Mod}_{R}(C)
for an open immersion
g^{\ast}\colon C\to D
.

We now show that rational localizations yield open immersion in
\mathrm{Sym}
for the completed solid category:

Lemma 2.37
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be a stably uniform analytic adic space, and
j\colon U:=\Spa(B,B^{+})\to X
a rational open.
Then
\hat{j}^{\ast}\colon\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X)\to\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(U)
is an open immersion in
\mathrm{Sym}
. In fact,
\hat{j}^{\ast}
is the base change of
j^{\ast}\colon\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X)\to\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(U)
along the functor
\alpha_{X}^{\ast}\colon\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X)\to\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X)
.

Lemma

2.35
and the proof below shows that
\alpha^{\ast}_{X}\circ j_{!}\cong\hat{j}_{!}\circ\alpha^{\ast}_{U}
for the left adjoints
j_{!},\hat{j}_{!}
of
j^{\ast},\hat{j}^{\ast}
.

Proof.

This follows from
Proposition

2.21
and
Lemma

2.35
: the morphism
(A^{\circ},A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(B^{\circ},B^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
is of
+
-finite type, and therefore

\D_{\hat{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}}(A^{\circ},A^{+})\otimes_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{\circ},A^{+})}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)^{\circ},\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+}(U))\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\D_{\hat{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)^{\circ},\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+}(U)).

(2.37.1)

We claim that the natural morphism
A\otimes_{A^{\circ}}B^{\circ}\to B
is an isomorphism in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{\circ},A^{+})
. Namely, by
[
1
, Theorem 1.6]
the claim is local on
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
, which reduces to the case that
A
is Tate, where the claim is clear. Given this claim, we can conclude

\D_{\hat{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}}(X)\otimes_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X)}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(U)\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\D_{\hat{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}}(U)

by tensoring (
Eq.

2.37.1
) over
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{\circ},A^{+})
with
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}((A^{\circ},A^{+}))
. From here
Lemma

2.35
implies the claim.
∎

We now establish the following version of Andreychev’s analytic descent theorem for the completed solid category:

Theorem 2.38
.

Let
(A,A^{+})
be a classical stably uniform analytic Huber pair, and
X:=\Spa(A,A^{+})
.

(i)

The functor
U\mapsto\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(U),\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+}(U))
on rational open subsets satisfies descent for the analytic topology.

(ii)

If for any rational open
U\subseteq X
each finite, étale
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-algebra is stably uniform (e.g.,
A
is sousperfectoid,
[
26
, Proposition 6.3.3.]
), then the functor
V\mapsto\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}_{V}(V),\mathcal{O}_{V}^{+}(V))
on the category of étale maps
V\to X
with
V
affinoid, satisfies descent for the étale topology.

Here, a morphism
V\to X
is étale if it is a composition of open immersions and finite étale maps (in the sense of
[
22
, Definition 7.1]
, generalized to our situation), and the étale topology is defined by jointly surjective étale morphisms.

Proof.

By definition of the étale topology, it suffices to show (i) and descent along finite étale maps
(A,A^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
. But by definition of the latter,
(B,B^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}=(B,A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
, which reduces to descent along the map
A\to B
of algebras in
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A,A^{+})
. Now,
A(\ast)\to B(\ast)
is descendable as a map of classical rings (
[
19
, Corollary 3.33]
). As it is moreover finite étale, this implies
B\cong A\otimes_{\underline{A(\ast)}}\underline{B(\ast)}
, and thus
A\to B
is descendable in
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A,A^{+})
because the functor
\D(A(\ast))\to\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A,A^{+})
is symmetric monoidal. Hence, (ii) is implied by (i). In order to show (i) we first note that if
X=\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}
is a finite open cover with
j_{i}\colon U_{i}\to X
rational open, then by
Lemma

2.37
the functors
\hat{j}^{\ast}_{i}\colon\D_{\hat{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}}(X)\to\D_{\hat{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}}(U_{i})
are open immersions in
\mathrm{Sym}
. Moreover, the collection
\hat{j}^{\ast}_{i},i=1,\ldots,n,
is jointly conservative. Indeed, this statement is equivalent to
\hat{j}_{1,!}(1_{U_{1}})\otimes\ldots\otimes\hat{j}_{n,!}(1_{U_{n}})=0
and thus is implied by
j_{1}(1_{U_{1}})\otimes\ldots\otimes j_{n}(1_{U_{n}})=0
(
[
1
, Proposition 4.12.(v)]
) using that
\alpha^{\ast}
commutes with the
!
-functors (
Lemma

2.37
). Thus, the assertion follows from the general descent theorem
[
7
, Proposition 5.5]
(or
[
1
, Proposition 4.13]
resp.
[
24
, Proposition 10.5]
).
∎

Theorem

2.38
allows us to give a reasonable definition of completed solid sheaves on every stably uniform analytic adic space:

Definition 2.39
.

Let
X
be a (classical) stably uniform analytic adic space. Then we set
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X)
as the inverse limit of
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A,A^{+})
over all affinoid opens
\Spa(A,A^{+})\subseteq X
, with
(A,A^{+})
a stably uniform analytic Huber pair.

2.5
Universal descent

In this section, we show that descent of
\D(-)
along a
steady
morphism
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
of analytic rings automatically implies descent after any base change. We discuss variants of this as well, e.g., for
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(-)
.

If
f\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a morphism of analytic rings, we will denote by
f^{\ast}(-):=\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}(-)\colon\D(\mathcal{A})\to\D(\mathcal{B})
the associated base change functor, and by
f_{\ast}
its right adjoint. Note that
f_{\ast}
is always conservative.

We recall that
f
is steady if for any morphism
g\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
of analytic rings with base change
f^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{B},g^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
, the natural transformation

f^{\ast}g_{\ast}\to g^{\prime}_{\ast}f^{\prime,\ast}

of functors
\D(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\to\D(\mathcal{B})
is an isomorphism (
[
25
, Proposition 12.14]
).

Lemma 2.40
.

Assume that
f\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a steady morphism of analytic rings and that modules descend along
f
, i.e., the natural functor
\D(A)\to\varprojlim\limits_{[n]\in\Delta}\D(\mathcal{B}^{n/\mathcal{A}})
is an equivalence, where
\mathcal{B}^{\bullet/\mathcal{A}}
denotes the Čech nerve for
f
. Then for any morphism
g\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
of analytic rings modules descend along
f^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
13
, Corollary 5.2.2.37.]
. We supplement the details using the notation of
[
13
, Corollary 5.2.2.37]
. Thus, we set
\mathcal{C}=\Delta
,
\chi\colon\Delta^{\vartriangleleft}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty},[n]\mapsto\D((\mathcal{B}^{\prime})^{n/\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})
with
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime})^{-1/\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}:=\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
, and similarly,
\chi^{\prime}([n]):=\D(\mathcal{B}^{n/\mathcal{A}})
. To construct the natural transformation
\rho\colon\chi\to\chi^{\prime}
, given componentwise by
g_{n,\ast}
for
g_{n}\colon\mathcal{B}^{n/\mathcal{A}}\to(\mathcal{B}^{\prime})^{n/\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
is the natural map, one can argue as follows: clearly, the
g^{\ast}_{n}
yield a natural transformation
\sigma\colon\chi^{\prime}\to\chi
(by naturality of
\D(-)
as a functor on the
\infty
-category of analytic rings). As for each morphism
[n]\to[m]
in
\Delta^{\vartriangleleft}
the induced morphism
\mathcal{B}^{n/\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{B}^{m/\mathcal{A}}
is steady (using
[
25
, Proposition 12.15]
), we can conclude
7
7

7

Let
E\to\Delta^{\vartriangleleft},E^{\prime}\to\Delta^{\vartriangleleft}
be the cocartesian fibrations classified by
\chi,\chi^{\prime}
. The natural transformation
\sigma
unstraightens to a functor
\kappa\colon E^{\prime}\to E
, which preserves cocartesian arrows. Using
[
13
, 7.3.2.6.]
the functor
\kappa
admits an adjoint
\lambda\colon E\to E^{\prime}
(relative to
\Delta^{\vartriangleleft}
) as this is true over each
[n]\in\Delta^{\vartriangleleft}
. Now, steadiness of
f
implies that
\lambda
preserves cocartesian arrows, and thus defines a natural transformation
\rho\colon\chi\to\chi^{\prime}
. By construction of
\lambda
,
\rho_{[n]}\colon\chi([n])\to\chi^{\prime}([n])
is given by the pushforward
g_{n,\ast}
as desired.
that the
g_{n,\ast}
assemble to a natural transformation
\rho\colon\chi\to\chi^{\prime}
. Now condition 1) in
[
13
, 5.2.2.37]
is the assumption that modules descend along
f
. Condition 2) is automatic as each
g_{n,\ast}
is conservative. Condition 3) is clear as
\D(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
has all limits and
g_{\ast}
preserves these. Condition 4) is again automatic because for each morphism
\alpha\colon[n]\to[m]
in
\Delta^{\vartriangleleft}
the functors
\chi([n])\to\chi([m])
resp.
\chi^{\prime}([n])\to\chi^{\prime}([m])
admit right adjoints given by
\ast
-pushforward, and clearly these right adjoints commute with
\rho
(which in component
[n]
is given by
g_{n,\ast}
). Thus,
[
13
, 5.2.2.37]
is applicable, and yields exactly that modules descend along
f^{\prime}
.
∎

Next assume that
f\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a morphism of adic analytic rings. By
Lemma

2.23
we know that
f
is steady if and only if
f
is adic. We get the following analog of
Lemma

2.40
for
\D(\hat{\mathcal{A}})
.

Lemma 2.41
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be an adic morphism of adic analytic rings, such that the natural functor
\D(\hat{\mathcal{A}})\to\varprojlim\limits_{[n]\in\Delta}\D(\hat{\mathcal{B}^{n/\mathcal{A}}})
is an equivalence. Then the same holds true after any base change
f^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{B}
of
f
along an adic morphism
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
of adic analytic rings.

Note that adicness of
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
implies by
Lemma

2.24
that all terms in the Čech nerves are adic analytic rings (and thus the assertion is well-defined).

Proof.

Replacing
\D((-))
by
\D(\hat{-})
we can follow the steps in
Lemma

2.40
. Using
Corollary

2.26
the existence of
\rho
follows in the same way. The only statement to check is that
\hat{f}_{\ast}\colon\D(\hat{\mathcal{B}})\to\D(\hat{\mathcal{A}})
is conservative for any morphism of adic analytic rings
f\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
. As
\alpha_{\ast}\hat{f}_{\ast}\cong f_{\ast}\alpha_{\ast}
(by
Lemma

2.7
and passage to right adjoints) this follows from conservativity of
f_{\ast}
and
\alpha_{\ast}
(
Lemma

2.5
).
∎

Remark 2.42
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be any morphism of stably uniform analytic adic spaces. Assume that all terms
Y^{n/X}
of the Čech nerve of
f
are again stably uniform (this is a serious assumption, cf.
Example

2.43
), and that
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}
satisfies descent for
f
. Then for any morphism
X^{\prime}\to X
of stably uniform analytic adic spaces (such that the terms in the Čech nerve of
f^{\prime}\colon Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
are again stably uniform) the modified quasi-coherent sheaves
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}
satisfy descent for
f^{\prime}
(by reducing to the affinoid case and then using the same argument as in
Lemma

2.40
,
Lemma

2.41
). Note that the steadiness assumption is automatically satisfied in this case because
X
is locally Tate.

Example 2.43
.

Let
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
be the completion of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})
. We mention here the well-known example that the Huber ring
A:=\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
(with
\otimes
refering to the solid or equivalently the Banach space tensor product) is not uniform even though each of the factors is. In fact, the uniform completion of
A
is the space
B:=C(\Gamma,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}})
of continuous functions on
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}=\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. The ring
A_{0}:={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
is a ring of definition of
A
(by definition of the Banach space tensor product), and the ring
B_{0}:=C(\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}})
is a ring of definition of
B
. Moreover,
B_{0}=B^{0}
because
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
is the ring of power bounded elements in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}}
. In particular,
B
is uniform. There exists the natural map

\Phi\colon A_{0}\to B_{0},a\otimes b\mapsto(\gamma\mapsto a\gamma(b)).

From the relation

\sum\limits_{\zeta\in\mu_{p^{n}}}\zeta^{x}=\begin{cases}p^{n},\ &x\in p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\\
0,\ &\textrm{ otherwise }\end{cases}

for
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
one can conclude that for
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
the continuous function

{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\mathrm{cycl}},\ x\mapsto\frac{1}{p^{h}}\sum\limits_{\zeta\in\mu_{p^{n}}}\zeta^{x-a}

is the characteristic function
\chi_{a,n}
of the clopen subset
a+p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}=\Gamma
. We can conclude that
\Phi
is injective with image exactly the closure of the subspace generated by the
p^{n}\chi_{a,n}
for
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
and
n\geq 0
(cf.
[
8
, Proposition I.3.4]
).
In particular,
\Phi
extends to an injection
A\to B
with dense image and this identifies
B
as the uniform completion of
A
. Concretely, for
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
and
n\geq 0
the elements

\frac{1}{p^{n}}\sum\limits_{\zeta\in\mu_{p^{n}}}\zeta^{-a}\otimes\zeta\in A

generate
A^{0}\subseteq A
as an
A_{0}
-module.

3
Sheaves on diamonds

Throughout this section we fix two primes
p
and
\ell
, where we explicitly allow the case
\ell=p
. In the following we construct and analyze different categories of
\ell
-adic sheaves on diamonds and small v-stacks over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. Most importantly, we discuss a version of the nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-sheaves defined in
[
16
]
in the case
\ell=p
. In fact, the basic definitions and results all work in the same way (similar to the fact that the basic results on étale
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-sheaves work the same as for
\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
), but the theory is lacking the required base-change result (for solid sheaves) to produce a good 6-functor formalism.
8
8

8

More precisely,
[
9
, Proposition VII.2.1]
needs the assumption
\ell\neq p
.
Nevertheless, nuclear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-sheaves will play an important role in the proof of descent for affinoid perfectoid spaces, whose tilt admits a morphism of finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
to a totally disconnected perfectoid space, in
Theorem

1.1
.

3.1
Étale sheaves

We fix a ring
\Lambda
such that
\ell^{n}\Lambda=0
for some
n\geq 0
. Recall that in
[
23
, Definition 14.13]
(and
[
23
, Lemma 17.1]
) a
v
-sheaf of
\infty
-categories
Y\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)
on small
v
-stacks is defined for any ring
\Lambda
such that
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)\cong\D(Y_{\mathrm{et}},\Lambda)
if
Y
is a strictly totally disconnected space (or more generally if
Y
is a locally spatial diamond, which has finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension). By descent from the strictly totally disconnected case the category
D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)
is equipped with a natural
t
-structure. More precisely, an object
M\in\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)
lies in
\D_{\geq 0}
(or
\D_{\leq 0}
) if and only this is true after pullback to all strictly totally disconnected spaces over
Y
. If
Y
is a locally spatial diamond such that
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)\cong\D(Y_{\mathrm{et}},\Lambda)
, then this recovers the usual
t
-structure on
\D(Y_{\mathrm{et}},\Lambda)
.
For a morphism
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
of small
v
-stacks the pullback functor
f^{\ast}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)
has a right adjoint
f_{\ast}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)
. In general, this right adjoint
f_{\ast}
is not easy to control. However, if
Y
is a diamond, then by descent from the strictly totally disconnected case the functor
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)\to\D(Y_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
is fully faithful (
[
23
, Proposition 14.10]
), and if
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
is a qcqs morphism of diamonds, then
f_{{\mathrm{qproet}},\ast}\colon\D(Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\to\D(Y_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
restricts to a functor
\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)\to\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)
(by the argument of the proof of
[
23
, Corollary 16.8.(ii)]
and
3.1
below). Thus,
f_{{\mathrm{qproet}},\ast}=f_{\ast}
on
\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)
.

In
[
23
, §16]
many base-change properties for étale sheaves on diamonds are discussed. In the following we need a slight generalization of
[
23
, Corollary 16.10]
to the case of diamonds that are not necessarily locally spatial:

Proposition 3.1
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}

be a cartesian diagram of diamonds. Assume that
f
is qcqs and that
g
is quasi-pro-étale. Then for
M\in\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)
the natural morphism

g^{\ast}f_{\ast}M\to f^{\prime}_{\ast}g^{\prime\ast}M

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

If
Y,X,Y^{\prime}
(and hence
X^{\prime}
) are locally spatial diamond, then
\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(-,\Lambda)\cong\D^{+}((-)_{\mathrm{et}},\Lambda)
by
[
23
, Proposition 14.15]
and the assertion follows from
[
23
, Corollary 16.10]
.
Using quasi-pro-étale descent of
\D_{\mathrm{et}}^{+}
we may formally reduce to the case that
Y,X
are spatial diamonds (even strictly totally disconnected perfectoid spaces). Let
h\colon Z\to Y^{\prime}
be a quasi-pro-étale surjection from a perfectoid space
Z
. Then
h
is qcqs and all terms in the Čech nerve for
h
are spatial diamonds (using
[
23
, Corollary 11.29]
). This formally reduces the assertion to the case that
Y^{\prime}
is as well spatial (more details on this reduction can be found in
[
17
, Proposition 3.3.7]
). This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

Proposition

3.1
holds for
any
ring
\Lambda
. The assumption
\ell^{n}\Lambda=0
is not necessary.

The following is an analog of
[
23
, Theorem 19.2]
that includes the case
\ell=p
. We make the additional assumption that
Y^{\prime}
and
Y
are diamonds to circumvent the failure of strong base change results mod
p
. In the following recall the definition of
j_{!}
from
[
23
, Definition 19.1]
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a proper morphism of diamonds, and let
j\colon U\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}Y
be an open immersion with pullback
g\colon U^{\prime}:=Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}U\to U
,
j^{\prime}\colon U^{\prime}\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}Y^{\prime}
. Then for any
A\in\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(U^{\prime},\Lambda)
the natural morphism
j_{!}g_{\ast}A\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}f_{\ast}j^{\prime}_{!}A
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof of
[
23
, Theorem 19.2]
goes through with a few changes: To avoid the use of
[
23
, Proposition 17.6]
, take a quasi-pro-étale cover
X\to Y
with
X
strictly totally disconnected (this is possible as
Y
is a diamond) and apply
Proposition

3.1
to reduce to the case
Y
strictly totally disconnected. We note that the reduction in
[
23
, Theorem 19.2]
to the case that
Y^{\prime}=\overline{X^{\prime}}^{/Y}
for some strictly totally disconnected space
X^{\prime}
over
Y=X
does not need
[
23
, Corollary 16.8.(ii)]
(or something similar) if the
v
-hypercover is constructed using quasi-pro-étale surjections by strictly totally disconnected spaces (which is possible as
Y^{\prime}
is assumed to be a diamond): the quasi-pro-étale site of
Y^{\prime}
is replete, and
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},B)\cong R\Gamma(Y^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{qproet}}},B)
for
B\in\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)
(this follows by quasi-pro-étale descent from
[
23
, Proposition 14.10]
). This implies the desired cohomological descent. The remaining steps in the proof, e.g.,
[
23
, Lemma 19.4]
, go through without change.
∎

The category of
\ell
-adic étale sheaves on a diamond
X
behaves particularly well if
X
is
\ell
-cohomologically bounded and the topology on
X
is not too wild. Regarding the second point,
[
23
]
usually works with (locally)
spatial
diamonds. For technical reasons we extend this definition slightly and work with
prespatial
diamonds instead. The reader is invited to ignore these subtleties and assume all prespatial diamonds to be spatial in the following.

Let us recall that a diamond
Y
is prespatial if there exists a spatial subdiamond
Y_{0}\subseteq Y
such that for all perfectoid fields
K
of characteristic
p
, the map
Y_{0}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})\to Y(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
is a bijection (
[
11
, Definition 3.1]
). We refer to
[
11
, Section 3.1]
for general properties of prespatial diamonds, e.g., stability of prespatial diamonds under fiber products. With this definition at hand, we now make the following definition, similar to
[
16
, Definition 2.1]
:

Definition 3.4
.

A prespatial diamond
Y
is called
\ell
-bounded
if there exists some
d\geq 0
such that for every
M\in\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
which is concentrated in degree
0
, the object

\displaystyle\Gamma(Y,M):=\Hom_{\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})}(\mathbb{F}_{\ell},M)\in\D(\mathbb{F}_{\ell})

is bounded to the right by
d
, i.e. lies in
\D_{\geq-d}=\D^{\leq d}
.

Remark 3.5
.

If
Y
is a spatial diamond then
\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{\ell})=\D^{+}(Y_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
and thus
\Gamma(Y,M)
just denotes the usual étale cohomology.

The following result helps detect
\ell
-bounded prespatial diamonds in some occations. In the next subsection we prove much more powerful
\ell
-boundedness results.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a qcqs quasi-pro-étale morphism of diamonds.

(i)

If
f
is separated, then
f_{\ast}
is
t
-exact.

(ii)

If
Y
is an
\ell
-bounded prespatial diamond, then
Y^{\prime}
is
\ell
-bounded and prespatial.

Proof.

Part (i) follows from
Proposition

3.1
and
[
23
, Remark 21.14]
. To prove (ii), note that it follows easily from
[
23
, Corollary 11.29]
that
Y^{\prime}
is prespatial. To prove
\ell
-boundedness of
Y^{\prime}
, we observe that
Y^{\prime}
is qcqs and
f
is locally separated by definition of quasi-pro-étale maps, hence we can assume that
f
is separated. But then the
t
-exactness of
f_{\ast}
reduces
\ell
-boundedness of
Y^{\prime}
to
\ell
-boundedness of
Y
.
∎

3.2
Cohomologically bounded maps

Fix a ring
\Lambda
such that
\ell^{n}\Lambda=0
for some
n\geq 0
. The goal of this subsection is to provide a relative version of the definition of
\ell
-bounded prespatial diamonds from
Definition

3.4
. This is more subtle than one might think at first, but we have found a definition that works well. In fact, the
p
-cohomological dimension of points in a diamond can increase after base change, and hence in the definition of a
p
-bounded morphism one needs to ask for bounds of these.

We briefly recall the
\ell
-adic cohomological dimension of points in a diamond. Let
Y
be a quasi-separated diamond, e.g., a prespatial diamond (see the previous subsection). As is proven in
[
23
, Proposition 21.9]
for each maximal point
y\in Y
the “residual” subdiamond
Y_{y}\subseteq Y
with
|Y_{y}|=\{y\}
is of the form
Y_{y}=\Spd(C_{y},\mathcal{O}_{C_{y}})/G_{y}
with
C_{y}
an algebraically closed perfectoid field of characteristic
p
, and
G_{y}
a profinite group acting continuously and faithfully on
C_{y}
. The pair
(C_{y},G_{y})
is unique up to (non-unique) isomorphism, and
Y_{y}
agrees with the stack quotient
[\Spd(C_{y},\mathcal{O}_{C_{y}})/G_{y}]
, i.e., the morphism
\Spd(C_{y},\mathcal{O}_{C_{y}})\times\underline{G_{y}}\to\Spd(C_{y},\mathcal{O}_{C_{y}})\times_{\Spd(\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Spd(C_{y},\mathcal{O}_{C_{y}})
is a monomorphism. The number
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}(y):=\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}(G_{y})
, with
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}(G_{y})
the
\ell
-cohomological dimension of
G_{y}
, is called the
\ell
-adic cohomological dimension of
y
. With this definition at hand, we can now come to the definition of
\ell
-bounded maps:

Definition 3.7
.

A morphism
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
of small
v
-stacks is called
\ell
-bounded if

1.

f
is locally separated and representable in prespatial diamonds (
[
11
, Definition 3.3]
), in particular
f
is qcqs,

2.

after pullback to every prespatial diamond,
f
has finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
(cf.
[
23
, Definition 21.7]
), and

3.

for all
X\to Y
with
X
a strictly totally disconnected space there exists some
d_{X}\geq 0
such that for all maximal points
y^{\prime}\in Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}X
the
\ell
-cohomological dimension
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}(y^{\prime})
is bounded by
d_{X}
(
[
23
, Definition 21.10]
).

Remarks 3.8
.

(i)

If
\ell\neq p
, then the first two conditions in the definition of an
\ell
-bounded morphism imply the third by
[
23
, Proposition 21.16]
.

(ii)

It is not known if the condition of having finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
can be checked
v
-locally on the source (cf.
[
23
, Section 21)]
). However, it is stable under pullback by
[
23
, Remark 21.8]
.

(iii)

Assume that
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
is a morphism between spatial diamonds. By
[
23
, Lemma 21.6]
the dimension of
f
as a morphism of spectral spaces, i.e., the supremum of the Krull dimensions of the fibers of
f
(
[
23
, Definition 21.1]
), is bounded by
\mathrm{dim.trg}
. Indeed, this reduces to the case that
Y=\Spa(C,C^{+})
, and then to the case that
Y^{\prime}
is a perfectoid space (as quasi-pro-étale surjections don’t change the Krull dimension of spatial diamonds).

It is a priori not clear that
\ell
-bounded maps are stable under composition. This is true and will be shown in
Lemma

3.13
below, after having established the necessary prerequisites. Let us start our discussion of
\ell
-bounded maps with the following observation, telling us that
\ell
-bounded maps have finite
\ell
-cohomological dimension in a suitable sense. This can be seen as a version of
[
23
, Theorem 22.5]
and
[
11
, Corollary 3.9]
.

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
be an
\ell
-bounded morphism of small
v
-stacks. Then pushforward along
f
has locally bounded cohomological dimension.

More precisely: After pullback along any
X\to Y
with
X
a diamond, the pushforward
f^{\prime}_{\ast}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}^{+}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)\to\D_{\mathrm{et}}^{+}(X,\Lambda)
along the base change
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}:=Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}X\to X
has finite cohomological dimension (with bound potentially depending on
X
).

If
\ell\neq p
, then by
[
11
, Corollary 3.9]
the pushforward
f_{\ast}
has cohomological dimension bounded by
3\cdot\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
. Note that in this case
f_{\ast}
commutes with base change by
[
23
, Proposition 17.6]
as
f
is qcqs.

Proof.

We may assume that
Y
is a diamond, and then by
Proposition

3.1
that
Y
is a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space. We change notation and assume that
f\colon X\to Y
is an
\ell
-bounded morphism with
Y
a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space. As
f
is locally separated and quasi-compact, we may assume that
X
is separated. Let
d\geq 1
be such that
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}(x)\leq d
for all maximal points
x\in X
. We claim that
f_{\ast}\colon\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)=\D^{+}(Y_{\mathrm{et}},\Lambda)
has a cohomological amplitude bounded in terms of
d
and
\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
(in fact
d+2\cdot\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
). This statement can be checked on stalks and hence we may assume that
Y=\Spa(C,C^{+})
for some algebraically closed, perfectoid field of characteristic
p
and
C^{+}\subseteq C
an open and bounded valuation subring. Using that
X\to\overline{X}^{/Y}
is quasi-pro-étale and separated, we may by
Lemma

3.6
replace
X
by
\overline{X}^{/Y}
and assume that
f
is proper (note that these reductions did not change
\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
or the cohomological bound
d
on the cohomological dimension of all maximal fields).
As in
[
23
, Theorem 22.5.]
, or
[
11
, Proposition 3.7]
, we may assume that
A\in\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(X,\Lambda)
is concentrated in degree
0
and
j^{\ast}A=0
, where
j\colon f^{-1}(U)\to X
is the base change of the open immersion
U\to Y
with complement the unique closed point of
Y
. More precisely, the use of
[
23
, Theorem 19.2]
is replaced by
Proposition

3.3
. If
X
is a spatial diamond, then as in
[
23
, Theorem 22.5.]
we may apply
[
23
, Proposition 21.11]
and conclude that
R^{i}f_{\ast}(A)=0
for
i>d+\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
.

It remains to handle the more general case that
X
is only
pre
spatial. But note that the above argument proves the theorem in the general case that
f
is proper and representable in spatial diamonds (with bound
d_{X}+\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
for
d_{X}
a cohomological bound of the cohomological dimension of all maximal points in the base change to the strictly totally disconnected space
X
). Thus the argument of
[
11
, Proposition 3.7]
applies without change (and yields the bound
d_{X}+2\cdot\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
), proving the prespatial case as well.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

In the setting of
Proposition

3.9
, if
\ell\neq p
then
f
has
\ell
-cohomological dimension
\leq 3\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
by
[
11
, Proposition 3.7]
and this constant is independent of base-change. However, in the case
\ell=p
it is unclear to us if there is a
p
-cohomological bound on
f
that works globally (i.e. after every base-change).

If
f\colon Y^{\prime}=\Spd(C^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{C^{\prime}})/G\to Y=\Spd(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
is a morphism of prespatial diamonds with
C\subseteq C^{\prime}
algebraically closed perfectoid fields and
\mathrm{cd}_{p}G,\mathrm{dimtrg}(f)
finite, then for
X=\Spd(D,\mathcal{O}_{D})\to Y
with
D
another algebraically closed perfectoid field, it is not clear to us if the cohomological dimension of all maximal points of
X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}
can be bounded by
\mathrm{cd}_{p}G
. It can nevertheless be bounded by
\mathrm{cd}_{p}G+1
(because fields in characteristic
p
have
p
-cohomological dimension
\leq 1
by
[
21
, Proposition 6.5.10]
).

The next result relates the notion of
\ell
-bounded maps to the notion of
\ell
-bounded prespatial diamonds from
Definition

3.4
, providing the expected compatibility:

Proposition 3.11
.

Let
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a locally separated morphism of prespatial diamonds such that
\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)<\infty
and
Y
is
\ell
-bounded. Then
f
is
\ell
-bounded if and only if
Y^{\prime}
is
\ell
-bounded.

Proof.

Assume that
Y^{\prime}
is
\ell
-bounded. By
[
11
, Lemma 3.4.(v)]

f
is representable in prespatial diamonds. We have to check the third condition for an
\ell
-bounded map in
Definition

3.7
. Let
g\colon X\to Y
be a morphism from a strictly totally disconnected space
X
and let
X^{\prime}:=Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}X
with projections
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to X
and
g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
. Let
d\geq 0
such that
H^{i}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\mathcal{F})=0
for
i>d
and
\mathcal{F}\in\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)
, which are concentrated in degree
0
. Let
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
be a maximal point, and
y^{\prime}:=g^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\in Y^{\prime}
. Then
y^{\prime}\in Y^{\prime}
is a maximal point, and hence
\{y^{\prime}\}\subseteq|Y^{\prime}|
is the underlying space of a subdiamond
j\colon Y^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}\to Y^{\prime}
. As
Y^{\prime}
is quasi-separated,
Y^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}
is quasi-separated, and hence by
[
23
, Propsition 21.9]
of the form
Y^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}=\Spd(C_{y^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{C_{y^{\prime}}})/G_{y^{\prime}}
for an algebraically closed perfectoid field
C_{y^{\prime}}
of characteristic
p
and
G_{y^{\prime}}
a profinite group acting continuously and faithfully on
C_{y^{\prime}}
. We note that
j
is quasi-compact, separated and quasi-pro-étale (
[
23
, Corollary 10.6]
), and hence
j_{\ast}\colon\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}},\Lambda)\to\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)
has cohomological amplitude
0
, i.e., is
t
-exact, by
[
23
, Remark 21.14]
(resp.
Lemma

3.6
). This implies that
\mathrm{cd}_{\ell}(G_{y^{\prime}})\leq d
because étale cohomology of
Y^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}
identifies with continuous cohomology of
G_{y^{\prime}}
. Set
Z:=X\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}
and note that
X^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}}\subseteq Z\subseteq X^{\prime}
. Applying the same argument to
X^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}}\subseteq Z
it suffices to see that
Z
is
\ell
-bounded (with a bound depending only on
d
and
\mathrm{dim.trg}
). Now, the affinoid perfectoid space
Z^{\prime}:=Z\times_{Y^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}}\Spd(C_{y^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{y^{\prime}})\cong X\times_{Y}\Spd(C_{y^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{y^{\prime}})
is a
G_{y^{\prime}}
-torsor over
Z
, and hence it suffices to show that
Z^{\prime}
is
\ell
-bounded (with a bound depending only on
\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
). Note that for
h\colon Z^{\prime}\to X
we have
\mathrm{dim.trg}(h)\leq\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
. Hence, if
\ell\neq p
, we can apply
[
11
, Corollary 3.9]
and the fact that
X
is strictly totally disconnected to conclude that
Z^{\prime}
is
\ell
-bounded (with bound
3\mathrm{dim.trg}(h)\leq 3\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
). If
\ell=p
, then we can apply
Lemma

3.12
below and conclude that
Z^{\prime}
is
\ell
-bounded (with bound
3+\mathrm{dim.trg}(h)\leq 3+\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
). This finishes the proof that
f
is
\ell
-bounded.

Assume conversely that
f
is
\ell
-bounded. As
Y
is
\ell
-bounded and
Y^{\prime}
prespatial, it suffices to show that
f_{\ast}\colon\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)\to\D^{+}_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\Lambda)
has finite cohomological dimension. This is implied by
Propositions

3.1
and
3.9
.
∎

The previous result made use of the following computation in the case
\ell=p
, which is of independent interest to us and is therefore extracted here:

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
X
be an affinoid perfectoid space which admits a map
f\colon X^{\flat}\to Z
to a totally disconnected space
Z
with
\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)<\infty
. Then
X
is
p
-bounded and the constant
d
in
Definition

3.4
can be chosen to be
d=\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)+3
.

Proof.

We may replace
X
by
X^{\flat}
and hence assume that
X
is of characteristic
p
. We first deal with the case that
X=Z
is totally disconnected. Let
\pi\colon X_{\mathrm{et}}\to\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{et}}
be the natural morphism of sites. As
\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{et}}
has cohomological dimension
0
(see
[
28
, Lemma 0A3F]
), it suffices to show that
R^{i}\pi_{\ast}(\mathcal{F})=0
for
i>2
and any static
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-sheaf on
X_{\mathrm{et}}
. This can be checked on stalks, and hence we may assume that
X=\Spa(K,K^{+})
is a perfectoid affinoid field in characteristic
p
, i.e.,
K
is a perfectoid field of characteristic
p
and
K^{+}\subseteq K
an open and bounded valuation subring. We may write
(K,K^{+})
as a (completed) filtered colimit of perfectoid affinoid fields
(L,L^{+})
and therefore reduce (via
[
23
, Proposition 14.9]
) to the case that
K^{+}
has finite Krull dimension. Let
j\colon U\to X
be the complement of the closed point. Then
U=\Spa(K,\widetilde{K}^{+})
for some valuation ring
K^{+}\subseteq\widetilde{K}^{+}
. By
[
23
, Lemma 21.13]
the fiber
\mathcal{G}:=[\mathcal{F}\to Rj_{\ast}j^{\ast}\mathcal{F}]
lies in
\D^{[0,1]}(X_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{F}_{p})
. By
[
23
, Proposition 21.15]
we can conclude that
H^{i}(X_{\mathrm{et}},\mathcal{G})=0
for
i\geq 3
because
\Spa(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
has
p
-cohomological dimension
\leq 1
(using
\Spa(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})_{\mathrm{et}}\cong\Spec(K)_{\mathrm{et}}
and
[
21
, Proposition 6.5.10]
). This implies that
H^{i}(X_{\mathrm{et}},\mathcal{F})\cong H^{i}(U_{\mathrm{et}},\mathcal{F}_{|U})
for
i\geq 3
. By induction this reduces to the case
X=\Spa(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
, in which case
H^{i}(X_{\mathrm{et}},\mathcal{F})=0
for
i\geq 2
.

Now assume that
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
is a general affinoid perfectoid space of characteristic
p
and that
f\colon X\to Z
is a morphism to a totally disconnected space
Z=\Spa(R,R^{+})
with
\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)<\infty
. As we have proven that
Z
is
p
-bounded, it suffices to show that
f_{\ast}\colon\D^{+}(X_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D^{+}(Z_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{F}_{p})
has cohomological dimension bounded by
1+\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
. Using again that fields of characteristic
p
have cohomological dimension
\leq 1
(
[
21
, Proposition 6.5.10]
) it is clear from the definition that
f
is a
p
-bounded morphism (as is any morphism between
affinoid
perfectoid spaces with finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
). Thus, the assertion follows from
Proposition

3.9
and going through the proof yields the bound
1+\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)
. This finishes the proof.
∎

With the previous results at hand we can finally check that
\ell
-bounded maps are stable under composition:

Lemma 3.13
.

\ell
-bounded morphisms of small
v
-stacks are stable under composition and base change.

Proof.

Stability under base change is clear by definition. Stability under composition follows from
Proposition

3.11
. More precisely, assume that
f\colon Y\to X,g\colon Z\to Y
are
\ell
-bounded morphisms. We can assume that
X
,
Y
and
Z
are prespatial diamonds. Then
\mathrm{dim.trg}(f\circ g)\leq\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)+\mathrm{dim.trg}(g)
by
[
23
, Lemma 21.3]
and clearly
f\circ g
is representable in prespatial diamonds. To check the third condition for an
\ell
-bounded morphism in
Definition

3.7
we may assume that
X
is strictly totally disconnected, and hence
\ell
-bounded. Then by
Proposition

3.11

Y
is
\ell
-bounded, which then implies by
Proposition

3.11
that
Z
is
\ell
-bounded. Applying
Proposition

3.11
again shows that
f\circ g
is
\ell
-bounded as desired.
∎

3.3
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves

In the following we bring the main definitions and results from
[
16
]
to our situation of interest, allowing explicitly the case
\ell=p
. Recall the definition of
\ell
-bounded (pre)spatial diamonds from
Definition

3.4
.

From now on we fix a static ring
\Lambda
, which is an adic and profinite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra. Then
\Lambda\cong\varprojlim_{n}\Lambda/I^{n}
for finite rings
\Lambda/I^{n}
and a finitely generated ideal of definition
I\subseteq\Lambda
containing
\ell
. We will abuse notation and write again
\Lambda
for the pro-étale sheaf
\varprojlim_{n}\Lambda/I^{n}
with inverse limit formed on the quasi-pro-étale site of a locally spatial diamond.

Before we can define nuclear
\Lambda
-sheaves on
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds, we need to study solid and
\omega_{1}
-solid
\Lambda
-sheaves as considered in
[
9
, Section VII.1]
and
[
16
, Section 2]
. In the following, a quasi-pro-étale map
U\to X
of spatial diamonds is called basic if it can be written as a cofiltered inverse limit of étale, quasi-compact and separated maps
U_{i}\to X
(
[
16
, Definition 2.2]
). We note that by
[
23
, Definition 10.1.(i)]
quasi-pro-étale maps of diamonds are locally separated by definition, and hence the basic quasi-pro-étale maps form a basic for
X_{\mathrm{qproet}}
.

Definition 3.14
.

Let
X
be a spatial diamond.

(a)

For every basic quasi-pro-étale map
U=\varprojlim_{i}U_{i}\to X
(see
[
16
, Definition 2.2]
) we denote
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]:=\varprojlim_{i}\Lambda[U_{i}]\in\D(X_{{\mathrm{qproet}}},\Lambda)
.
9
9

9

The quasi-pro-étale site is defined in
[
23
, Definition 14.1.(ii)]
.

(b)

A quasi-pro-étale sheaf
\mathcal{M}\in\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
is called
solid
if for all basic quasi-pro-étale
U\to X
the natural map

\displaystyle\IHom(\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U],\mathcal{M})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\IHom(\Lambda[U],\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism. We denote by
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\subseteq\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
the full subcategory spanned by the solid sheaves.

(c)

Assume
X
is
\ell
-bounded. A solid sheaf
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is called
\omega_{1}
-solid
if for every
\omega_{1}
-filtered colimit
U=\varprojlim_{i}U_{i}
of basic objects in
X_{\mathrm{qproet}}
the natural map

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{i}\Gamma(U_{i},\mathcal{M})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\Gamma(U,\mathcal{M})

is an isomorphism. We denote by
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
the full subcategory spanned by the
\omega_{1}
-solid objects.

(d)

An object
M\in\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
is called complete if it is
I
-adically complete for some finitely generated ideal of definition
I\subseteq\Lambda
.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
X
be a spatial diamond.

(i)

The category
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is stable under limits and colimits in
\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
and contains all étale sheaves.

(ii)

\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is compactly generated and a collection of compact generators is given by
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
for w-contractible basic
U\in X_{\mathrm{qproet}}
. Moreover, for each basic quasi-pro-étale
U\to X
the object
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
is static.

(iii)

The
t
-structure on
\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
.

(iv)

The composition
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\to\D(X_{v},\Lambda)
is fully faithful, and
X\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is a
v
-sheaf of
\infty
-categories.

(v)

The inclusion
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
admits a left adjoint
(-)_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
such that
(\Lambda[U])_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}=\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
for
U\to X
basic quasi-pro-étale.

(vi)

There exists a unique closed symmetric monoidal structure
-\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}-
on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
such that
(-)_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
is symmetric monoidal. We have
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U^{\prime}]\cong\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U\times_{X}U^{\prime}]
for
U,U^{\prime}\to X
basic quasi-pro-étale.

(vii)

If
M,N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{-}(X,\Lambda)
are complete, then
M\otimes_{\Lambda}^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}N
is complete.

(viii)

For
M\in\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
and
N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
we have
\IHom_{\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)}(M,N)\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
.

(ix)

The natural functor
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}))
is an equivalence.

In
[
9
, Defintion VII.1.17]
,
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is defined as
\Lambda
-modules in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
for any solid
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
. If
\Lambda
is an adic and profinite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra, then
Proposition

3.15
shows that the neat characterization
Definition

3.14
holds.

Proof.

We first assume that
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
. We first note that our (a priori different) definition
Definition

3.14
agrees with
[
9
, Definition VII.1.10]
. Indeed, if
M\in\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
has solid cohomology objects in the sense of
[
9
, Definition VII.1.1]
then by
[
9
, VII.1.12]

M
is solid in the sense of
Definition

3.14
. Moreover, the full subcategory
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
defined in
[
9
, Definition VII.1.10]
is stable under all colimits and limits in
\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
(by
[
9
, Theorem VII.1.3]
) and contains
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[U]
for any basic quasi-pro-étale
U\to X
. Let
N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
. Then
N=\varinjlim_{i}\Lambda[U_{i}]
is a colimit in
\D(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
for basic quasi-pro-étale morphisms
U_{i}\to X
with
U_{i}

w
-contractible, as these form a basis of
X_{\mathrm{qproet}}
. This implies that
N
is a retract of
\varinjlim_{i}\Lambda_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[U_{i}]
, and thus contained in
\mathcal{C}
. This finishes the claim that
\mathcal{C}=\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
.
Now, the assertions follow from
[
9
, Theorem VII.1.3]
,
[
9
, Proposition VII.1.13]
and
[
9
, Proposition VII.1.14]
,
[
9
, Proposition VII.1.8]
,
[
9
, Proposition VII.1.11]
and
[
16
, Proposition 2.8]
(which does not use the assumption
\ell\neq p
). This finishes the case that
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
.

Let now
\Lambda
be a general adic and profinite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra. We claim that the natural map

{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[U]\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\Lambda\to\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]

is an isomorphism for any basic quasi-pro-étale
U\to X
. Now,
\Lambda=U\to X
is represented (as a sheaf on
X_{\mathrm{qproet}}
) by a basic quasi-pro-étale morphism over
X
because
\Lambda
is adic and profinite. In particular, we see that
\Lambda
(being solid) is a retract of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[\Lambda]
. We know that

{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[U]\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[\Lambda]\cong{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[U\times_{X}\Lambda],

which implies the claim by the passage to a retract (and it implies that
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
is static).
From here it is formal that an object
C\in\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
is solid if and only if its underlying object
C_{|{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}\in\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is solid. Indeed, if
C
is solid, then by the same argument as before
C
is a retract of a colimit of
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
’s with
U\to X
basic quasi-pro-étale, and each of the
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
is solid. Conversely, if
C_{|{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}
is solid, then

\IHom_{\Lambda}(\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U],C)\cong\IHom_{\Lambda}(\Lambda\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[U],C)\cong\IHom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[U],C_{|{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}})\cong\Gamma(U,C)

as desired.

We can conclude that
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\subseteq\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
is stable under all colimits and limits, and that it contains all (static) sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules, which are pulled back from
X_{\mathrm{et}}
. Similarly, the claim on the
t
-structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
follows as well as the existence of the desired left adjoint
(-)_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
. To get the symmetric monoidal structure
-\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}-
it suffices to show that if
C,D\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
then
\IHom_{\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)}(C,D)
is contained in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
. By stability of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
under limits it suffices to handle the case that
C=\Lambda\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}C^{\prime}
for some
C^{\prime}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
. Then it follows from the same assertion for
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
(implicitly proven in
[
9
, Proposition VII.1.14]
).

Assume that
M,N\in\D^{-}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
are complete. From the case
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
we can conclude that for each
n\geq 0
the tensor product
M\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\Lambda^{\otimes n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}N
is
\ell
-adically complete. This implies that
M\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}N
is
\ell
-adically complete as the
\ell
-adic completion commutes with uniformly right-bounded geometric realizations. Using a similar argument for preservation of completedness under geometric realizations, we can reduce first to the case that
\Lambda=\mathbb{F}_{\ell}[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]]
for some
r\geq 0
, and then to the case that
r=1
. For
\Lambda=\mathbb{F}_{\ell}[[x]]
the same argument as in
[
16
, Proposition 2.8]
applies.

The final claim
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}))
follows from the observation that both embed fully faithfully into
\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
with generators
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[U]\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\Lambda\cong\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
.
Now, we can conclude that
X\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is a
v
-sheaf of
\infty
-categories on spatial diamonds, e.g., using
[
13
, Corollary 5.2.2.37]
.
∎

We now turn to the better behaved subcategory of
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves, which was introduced (for
\ell\neq p
) in
[
16
]
. We note that the compact generators of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
are much simpler than the ones of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
. In particular,
\Lambda\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is compact, while in general
\Lambda\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is not.

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond.

(i)

The category
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is stable under colimits and countable limits in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
and contains all étale sheaves.

(ii)

\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is compactly generated and a collection of compact generators is given by
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
for sequential limits
U=\varprojlim_{n}U_{n}
with all
U_{n}\to X
being étale, quasicompact and separated.

(iii)

The
t
-structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

(iv)

The tensor product
-\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}-
in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
restricts to a closed symmetric monoidal structure on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

(v)

If
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is
\omega_{1}
-compact, and
N\in\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
compact, then
\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(M,N)=\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)}(M,N)
.

(vi)

\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}})
.

Proof.

If
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
then this - except for the claim on internal Hom’s - is proven in
[
16
, Proposition 2.5]
(note that the proof of that result uses nowhere that
\ell\neq p
). Thus, let
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
be
\omega_{1}
-compact, and let
N\in\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
be compact. Then
M
is a countable colimit of compact objects, and by stability of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
under countable limits we may therefore assume that
M=\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
is compact. As
N
is compact, we may reduce to the case that it is a countable inverse limit of étale sheaves, and thus we may assume that
N
is an étale sheaf. In this case, one checks directly that
\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)}(M,N)
is
\omega_{1}
-solid. This finishes the case
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
.

Back in the case that
\Lambda
is a static adic and profinite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra we observe that
\Lambda\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
and that an object
C\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is
\omega_{1}
-solid if and only if
C_{|\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}}\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is
\omega_{1}
-solid. Moreover,
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[U]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell,{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}}\Lambda
by the proof of
Proposition

3.15
. It follows that
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is the same as the category of
\Lambda
-modules in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})_{\omega_{1}}
by
Proposition

3.15
. All assertions now follow from the case
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
.
∎

We note the following stability of
(\omega_{1}-)
solid sheaves under pullback and pushforward along maps of spatial diamonds.

Lemma 3.17
.

Let
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism spatial diamonds, with associated morphisms
f_{\mathrm{qproet}}\colon X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to X_{\mathrm{qproet}},f_{v}\colon X^{\prime}_{v}\to X_{v}
on sites.

(i)

f^{\ast}_{\mathrm{qproet}}\colon\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\to\D(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda),f^{\ast}_{v}\colon\D(X_{v},\Lambda)\to\D(X_{v},\Lambda)
restrict to the same
t
-exact, symmetric monoidal functor

f^{\ast}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda),

which preserves
\omega_{1}
-solid objects (if
X^{\prime},X
are
\ell
-bounded).

(ii)

If
f
is quasi-pro-étale, then
f_{{\mathrm{qproet}},\ast}\colon\D(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\to\D(X,\Lambda)
restricts to a colimit-preserving functor

f_{\ast}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda),

which preserves
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves (if
X^{\prime},X
are
\ell
-bounded).

(iii)

If
\ell\neq p
, then
f_{{\mathrm{qproet}},\ast}\colon\D(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\to\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda),f_{v,\ast}\colon\D(X^{\prime}_{v},\Lambda)\to\D(X_{v},\Lambda)
restrict to the same colimit-preserving functor

f_{v,\ast}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda),

which preserves
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves (if
X^{\prime},X
are
\ell
-bounded).

Proof.

The first assertion follows from
[
9
, Proposition VII.1.8]
(and the proof of
[
16
, Proposition 2.6]
for the preservation of
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves). The third assertion (for
f_{v,\ast}
) is
[
9
, Proposition VII.2.1]
. Using
[
23
, Corollary 16.8]
,
[
23
, Corollary 16.9]
and
[
23
, Corollary 16.10]
the same argument implies the second assertion and the case
f_{{\mathrm{qproet}},\ast}
in the third assertion. The preservation of
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves follows as in
[
16
, Proposition 2.6]
(replacing the use of
[
23
, Corollary 16.8.(ii)]
by
[
23
, Corollary 16.8.(i)]
for the second part).
∎

Corollary 3.18
.

(i)

The functors
X\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
,
X\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
are hypercomplete quasi-pro-étale sheaves on the site of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds.
10
10

10

By
Lemma

3.6
being
\ell
-bounded ascents along under quasi-compact, separated quasi-pro-étale maps

(ii)

If
\ell\neq p
, then
X\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
,
X\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
are hypercomplete
v
-sheaves on the site of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds.

Proof.

The case for
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is clear by
[
9
, Proposition VII.1.8]
.
Thanks to
Lemma

3.17
the proof of
[
16
, Corollary 2.7]
implies both assertions for
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves.
∎

3.4
Overconvergent solid sheaves

As in
Section

3.3
we fix a prime
\ell
and a static adic and profinite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
\Lambda
.

We now discuss the definition of overconvergent objects in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
following
[
16
, Section 6]
, although with some differences. Most notably, we don’t require that overconvergent sheaves are nuclear (in the sense of
[
16
, Definition 3.1]
). Note that if
T
is a profinite set, then we can consider its quasi-pro-étale site
T_{\mathrm{qproet}}
11
11

11

For example, defined as the slice
\ast_{\mathrm{proet}}/T
with
\ast_{\mathrm{proet}}
the pro-étale site of a point,
[
4
, Section 4.3]
.
, and the full subcategories
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(T,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(T,\Lambda)\subseteq\D(T_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
similarly defined as in
Definition

3.14
. These categories satisfy the same properties as in
Proposition

3.15
and
Proposition

3.16
(this can be checked, e.g., by taking the product of
T
with
\Spa(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
for
C
a perfectoid algebraically closed field in characteristic
p
). In particular, we can call a morphism
S\to T
of profinite sets “basic quasi-pro-étale” if
S=\varprojlim_{i}S_{i}\to T
with
S_{i}\to T
a local isomorphism for
S_{i}
a profinite set.

We recall that if
X
is a spatial diamond, then there exists a natural morphism
\pi=\pi_{X}\colon X_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}
of sites such that
\pi^{-1}(T)=T\times_{\pi_{0}(X)}X
(
[
18
, Definition 5.5]
).

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
X
be a spatial diamond with morphism of sites
\pi\colon X_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}
.

(i)

The functor
\pi^{-1}\colon\widetilde{\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}}\to\widetilde{X_{\mathrm{qproet}}}
commutes with limits, and
\pi_{\ast}\circ\pi^{-1}=\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}}}
. In fact, if
V\in X_{{\mathrm{qproet}}}
is qcqs, then
\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{F})(V)=\mathcal{F}(\pi_{0}(V))
for any sheaf of sets
\mathcal{F}
on
\pi_{0}(X)
.

(ii)

The functor
\pi^{-1}\colon\widetilde{\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}}\to\widetilde{X_{\mathrm{qproet}}}
admits a left adjoint
\pi_{\natural}\colon\widetilde{X_{\mathrm{qproet}}}\to\widetilde{\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}}
such that
\pi_{\natural}(V\to X)=(\pi_{0}(V)\to\pi_{0}(X))
if
V\in X_{\mathrm{qproet}}
is qcqs.

Proof.

The first assertion is
[
18
, Lemma 5.7]
, and the second is implied by
[
18
, Lemma 5.4]
.
∎

In the strictly totally disconnected case stronger properties hold true (generalizing the results in
[
16
, Lemma 6.7]
).

Lemma 3.20
.

Let
X
be a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space.

(i)

If
X
is strictly totally disconnected, then
\pi^{\ast}\colon\D(\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\to\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
is fully faithful, and preserves (
\omega_{1}
-)solid sheaves and all colimits and limits. Its restriction,
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
is symmetric monoidal.

(ii)

The functor
\pi_{\ast}\colon\D(X_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\to\D(\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)
is
t
-exact and preserves (
\omega_{1}
-)solid sheaves.

(iii)

The functor
\pi^{\ast}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
admits a left adjoint
\pi_{\natural}
, which is symmetric monoidal and preserves
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves. Moreover, if
U\to X
is basic quasi-pro-étale, then
\pi_{\natural}(\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U])\cong\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi_{0}(U)]
.

(iv)

For
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
and
N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)
the natural map

\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)}(M,\pi^{\ast}N)\to\pi^{\ast}\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)}(\pi_{\natural}M,N)

is an isomorphism. Similarly, with
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(-,\Lambda)
replaced by
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(-,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

Proof.

Note that
Lemma

3.19
implies that
\Lambda
is pulled back from
\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}
, and thus
\pi^{\ast}=\pi^{-1}
(on static sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules). Hence, for fully faithfulness in the first assertion it suffices (by
Lemma

3.19
) to see that
\pi_{\ast}
is exact on static sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules. This can be checked on extremally disconnected profinite sets
T
over
\pi_{0}(X)
. For such
T
the pullback
Z:=\pi^{-1}(T)=T\times_{\pi_{0}(X)}X
is
w
-contractible in
X_{\mathrm{qproet}}
, i.e., each pro-étale cover of
Z
splits. Preservation of (
\omega_{1}
-)solid sheaves by
\pi^{\ast}
follows from commutation of
\pi^{-1}
with limits and colimits by checking on the generators of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)
resp.
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
provided by
Proposition

3.15
resp.
Proposition

3.16
(translated to
\pi_{0}(X)
).

That
\pi^{\ast}
is symmetric monoidal can be checked on generators where it follows by
Proposition

3.15
from the fact that

(T\to\pi_{0}(X))\mapsto T\times_{\pi_{0}(X)}X

commutes with fiber products. Here,
T
is assumed to be profinite.

For (ii) we have to check that
\pi_{\ast}
preserves (
\omega_{1}
-)solid sheaves. Let
N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
and
T\to\pi_{0}(X)
profinite, written as
T=\varprojlim_{i}T_{i}
with
T_{i}
profinite and
T_{i}\to\pi_{0}(X)
a local isomorphism. Set
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[T]:=\varprojlim_{i}\Lambda[T_{i}]
on
\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}
. Note that
\pi^{\ast}(\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[T])=\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi^{-1}(T)]
as
\pi^{-1}
preserves limits. Then we can conclude

\displaystyle\IHom(\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[T],\pi_{\ast}N)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}(\IHom(\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi^{-1}T]),N))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}(\IHom(\Lambda[\pi^{-1}T],N))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}(\IHom(\pi^{-1}(\Lambda[T]),N))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\IHom(\Lambda[T],\pi_{\ast}(N))

using that
N
is solid and the formal identity
\pi^{-1}(\Lambda[T])\cong\Lambda[\pi^{-1}T]
. For for any cofiltered limit of maps
T_{j}\to\pi_{0}(X)
with
T_{j}
profinite we have

\Gamma(\varprojlim_{j}T_{j},\pi_{\ast}(N))\cong\Gamma(\pi^{-1}(\varprojlim_{j}T_{j}),N)\cong\Gamma(\varprojlim_{j}\pi^{-1}T_{j},N)

as
\pi^{-1}
preserves limits. From here it follows that
\pi_{\ast}
preserves
\omega_{1}
-solid objects.

The existence of
\pi_{\natural}
follows as
\pi^{-1}
preserves limits (or from
[
18
, Lemma 5.4]
). It follows formally from the formula
\pi^{-1}\mathcal{F}(V)=\mathcal{F}(\pi_{0}(V))
that
\pi_{\natural}(\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U])=\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi_{0}(U)]
for
U\to X
basic quasi-pro-étale. Using
[
16
, Lemma 5.8]
we can conclude that
\pi_{0}(U\times_{X}V)=\pi_{0}(U)\times_{\pi_{0}(X)}\pi_{0}(V)
for
U,V
basic quasi-pro-étale over
X
. This implies that
\pi_{\natural}
is symmetric monoidal by
Proposition

3.15
. We need to see that
\pi_{\natural}
preserves
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves. The explicit formula for
\pi_{\natural}\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
and the fact that
\pi_{0}(\varprojlim_{i}U_{i})\cong\varprojlim_{i}\pi_{0}(U_{i})
for étale, separated maps
U_{i}\to X
in a cofiltered system
\{U_{i}\}_{i}
shows that it suffices to show that
\pi_{\natural}
maps
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
to an
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaf of
U\to X
is étale quasi-compact and separated. This proven in
Lemma

3.21
.
The assertion (iv) follows formally from symmetric monoidality of
\pi_{\natural}
. In fact, the left hand side is right adjoint as a functor in
N
to
\pi_{\natural}(M\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}(-))
while the right hand side is right adjoint to
\pi_{\natural}(M)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}\pi_{\natural}(-)
.
∎

The proof of
Lemma

3.20
made use of following result about
\omega_{1}
-solidness of
\pi_{\natural}\Lambda[U]
. Be aware that even if
U\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}X
is an open immersion, the map
\pi_{0}(U)\to\pi_{0}(X)
is in general quite far from being an open immersion itself, which makes the
\omega_{1}
-solidness of
\pi_{\natural}\Lambda[U]=\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi_{0}(U)]
more subtle than one might think at first.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
j\colon U\to X
be a quasi-compact, separated, étale morphism to a strictly totally disconnected space
X
. Then
\pi_{\natural}\Lambda[U]=\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi_{0}(U)]\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)
is
\omega_{1}
-solid.

Proof.

Write
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
. As
X
is strictly totally disconnected, each point
u\in U
has a quasi-compact open neighborhood
V_{u}
, such that the map
V_{u}\to X
is an open immersion. Indeed, this follows from the definition of an étale morphism (
[
23
, Definition 6.2]
) using that each finite étale map onto some quasi-compact open in
X
will split. Hence, without loss of generality
j\colon U\to X
can be assumed to be a quasi-compact open immersion (using that
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves are stable under finite colimits). By
[
23
, Lemma 7.6]

U
is the intersection of subsets
\{|f|\leq 1\}
for varying
f\in A
. As
U\subseteq X
is quasi-compact open,
X\setminus U
is quasi-compact in the constructible topology. As any
\{|f|\leq 1\}
for
f\in A
is closed in the constructible topology, this implies that
U
is the finite intersection of subsets
U_{1}=\{|f_{1}|\leq 1\},\ldots,U_{r}=\{|f_{r}|\leq 1\}
with
f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}\in A
. By
[
18
, Lemma 5.8]

\pi_{0}(U)=\pi_{0}(U_{1})\times_{\pi_{0}(X)}\ldots\times_{\pi_{0}(X)}\pi_{0}(U_{r})
and thus
Proposition

3.15
,
Proposition

3.16
imply that we may assume that
U=U_{1}=\{|f|\leq 1\}
for
f:=f_{1}
. Let
\pi\in A
be a pseudo-uniformizer and set

W_{n}:=\{|f|<|\pi^{-1/p^{n}}|\}

for
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Each
W_{n}
is a spectral space, being closed in the constructible topology (
[
28
, 0902]
), and hence
\pi_{0}(W_{n})
is a profinite set. We claim:

1)

\pi_{0}(U),\pi_{0}(W_{n}),n\in\mathbb{N},
are subspaces of
\pi_{0}(X)
,

2)

\bigcap\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}W_{n}
is the closure of
U
,

3)

\pi_{0}(U)=\bigcap\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\pi_{0}(W_{n})
,

4)

for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
the subspace
\pi_{0}(U)
lies in the interior of
\pi_{0}(W_{n})\subseteq\pi_{0}(X)
.

The claims imply that
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi_{0}(U)]
is
\omega_{1}
-solid. Indeed, we may inductively construct quasi-compact open subsets
S_{n}\subseteq\pi_{0}(X)
with
\pi_{0}(U)\subseteq S_{n}\subseteq\pi_{0}(W_{n})
, and
S_{n+1}\to S_{n}
. Then
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi_{0}(U)]=\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Lambda[S_{n}]
is
\omega_{1}
-solid by
Proposition

3.16
.

To show 1) it suffices (because the
\pi_{0}
are profinite and the maps continuous) to show that
\pi_{0}(U)\to\pi_{0}(X),\pi_{0}(W_{n})\to\pi_{0}(X)
are injective. Now,
U
is open, and thus stable under generalization while the
W_{n}
are closed, hence stable under specializations. This implies that if
Z\subseteq X
is a connected component, then
Z\cap U
,
Z\cap W_{n}
are connected (if non-empty) because
Z
is a linearly ordered chain of points. This in turn implies injectivity.

As each
W_{n}
is closed, and
U\subseteq W_{n}
we see
\overline{U}\subseteq\bigcap\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}W_{n}
. The reverse implication may be checked after intersection with each connected component
Z
of
X
(because
\overline{U}
is the set of specializations of
U
by
[
28
, 0903]
). If
Z\cap U\neq
, then
\overline{U}\cap Z=Z
and similarly for the intersection with the
W_{n}
’s. Let
z
be the generic point of
Z
and assume that
Z\cap\overline{U}=\varnothing
or equivalently
Z\cap U=\varnothing
. Thus,
|f(z)|>1
and as
z
is a rank
1
valuation this implies that
z\notin\{|f|\leq|\pi^{-1/p^{n}}|\}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. This implies that
Z\cap W_{n}=\varnothing
as the set
\{|f|\leq|\pi^{-1/p^{n}}|\}
is stable under generalizations. Thus
Z\cap\bigcap\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}W_{n}=\varnothing
as well.

Assertion 3) follows from 2) because
\pi_{0}(U)=\pi_{0}(\overline{U})=\bigcap\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\pi_{0}(W_{n})
.

We are left with assertion 4). Fix a connected component
Z\subseteq X
with
Z\cap U\neq\varnothing
, i.e., the point
z\in\pi_{0}(X)
defined by
Z
lies in
\pi_{0}(U)
. We can write
Z=\bigcap\limits_{i\in I}f^{-1}(T_{i})
with
T_{i}\subseteq\pi_{0}(X)
running through the quasi-compact open neighborhoods of
z
and
f\colon|X|\to\pi_{0}(X)
the natural quotient map. Note that the
f^{-1}(T_{i})
are closed and open in
X
. Moreover, let us fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We know that
Z\subseteq W_{n}
as
\overline{U}\subseteq W_{n}
and the generic point of
Z
lies in
U
. Furthermore,
X\setminus W_{n}=\{|\pi^{-1/p^{n}}|\leq|f|\}
is a rational open subset of
X
. In particular,
X\setminus W_{n}
is compact in the constructible topology. As
X\setminus W_{n}\cap\bigcap\limits_{i\in I}f^{-1}(T_{i})=X\setminus W_{n}\cap Z=\varnothing
, we can conclude that there exists some
i_{0}
such that
X\setminus W_{n}\cap f^{-1}(T_{i_{0}})=\varnothing
, i.e.,
f^{-1}(T_{i_{0}})\subseteq W_{n}
. This implies that
z\in T_{i_{0}}\subseteq\pi_{0}(W_{n})
. In particular,
z
lies in the interior of
\pi_{0}(W_{n})
. This finishes the proof.
∎

With the previous results at hand, we can now introduce a well-behaved (see
Lemma

3.23
) notion of overconvergent sheaves on general spatial diamonds:

Definition 3.22
.

(a)

Let
X
be a strictly totally disconnected space with morphism of sites
\pi\colon X_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}
. An object
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is called overconvergent if
M\cong\pi^{\ast}N
for some
N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{proet}},\Lambda)
.

(b)

Let
X
be a spatial diamond. An object
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is called overconvergent if
f^{\ast}M
is overconvergent for any quasi-pro-étale morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
with
X^{\prime}
strictly totally disconnected.

We let
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
(resp.
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}_{\omega_{1}}\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
) denote the full subcategories spanned by the overconvergent objects (resp. the overconvergent and
\omega_{1}
-solid objects).

Lemma 3.23
.

Let
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism of strictly totally disconnected spaces.

(i)

If
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
is overconvergent, then so is
f^{\ast}M
.

(ii)

Conversely, if
f
is a quasi-pro-étale cover and
f^{\ast}M
is overconvergent, then so is
M
.

Proof.

The first claim follows by naturality in
X
of the morphism of sites
\pi\colon X_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}}
. For the converse we note that (using
[
18
, Lemma 5.8]
) it suffices to see that
X\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)
is a hypercomplete quasi-pro-étale sheaf on strictly totally disconnected spaces. Now
Corollary

3.18
implies that
T\mapsto\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(T,\Lambda)
is a quasi-pro-étale sheaf on profinite sets, in other words a condensed sheaf of
\infty
-categories on
\ast_{\mathrm{qproet}}
. As
X\mapsto\pi_{0}(X)
sends quasi-pro-étle covers, even
v
-covers, to quasi-pro-étale covers the claim follows.
∎

The next result summarizes the very good properties of the full subcategory of overconvergent objects.

Lemma 3.24
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond.

(i)

Overconvergent objects satisfy quasi-pro-étale hyperdescent on the big site of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds.

(ii)

The inclusion
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
admits a symmetric monoidal left adjoint
M\mapsto M_{\mathrm{oc}}
and a right adjoint
M\mapsto M^{\mathrm{oc}}
. In particular,
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}
is stable under all colimits and limits in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
.

(iii)

The inclusion
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}_{\omega_{1}}\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
admits a symmetric monoidal left adjoint
M\mapsto M_{\mathrm{oc}}
and a right adjoint
M\mapsto M^{\mathrm{oc}}
. In particular,
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}_{\omega_{1}}
is stable under all colimits and limits in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

(iv)

If
M,N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
and
N
is overconvergent, then
\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)}(M,N)
is overconvergent and naturally isomorphic to
\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)}(M_{\mathrm{oc}},N)
. Similarly, with
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
replaced by
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

(v)

If
U=\varprojlim\limits_{i\in I}U_{i}\to X
with
U_{i}\to X
étale, quasi-compact and separated, then the natural map
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]\to\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\overline{U}^{/X}]
induces an isomorphism
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]_{\mathrm{oc}}\cong\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\overline{U}^{/X}]
. In particular, the functor
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda),\ M\mapsto M_{\mathrm{oc}}
preserves compact objects.

(vi)

The functor
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda),\ M\mapsto M^{\mathrm{oc}}
preserves colimits and right bounded objects.

Proof.

Assertion (i) follows from
Lemma

3.23
and
Corollary

3.18
.
For assertion (ii) it suffices by the adjoint functor theorem to show that
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}
is stable under all colimits and limits in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
. By
Proposition

3.15
and (i) we may then assume that
X
is a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space. Then the assertion follows from
Lemma

3.20
. We note that in this case we have
M_{\mathrm{oc}}=\pi^{\ast}\pi_{\natural}(M)
and
M^{\mathrm{oc}}=\pi^{\ast}\pi_{\ast}(M)
for
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
. As in
Lemma

3.20
symmetric monoidality of
(-)_{\mathrm{oc}}
implies assertion (iv).

For assertion (iii) we may again reduce to the case that
X
is strictly totally disconnected. Indeed, for the existence of
(-)^{\mathrm{oc}}
this follows by stability of overconvergent sheaves under colimits (which can be checked on a strictly totally disconnected cover), and the existence of
(-)_{\mathrm{oc}}
can be descended from the strictly totally disconnected case as there
(-)_{\mathrm{oc}}
commutes with pullback as will be proved in
Lemma

3.25
.
Then the assertion follows again from
Lemma

3.20
as
\pi_{\natural},\pi^{\ast},\pi_{\ast}
all preserve
\omega_{1}
-solid sheaves.

Let us note that if
U\to X
is quasi-pro-étale, then
\overline{U}^{/X}\to X=\overline{X}^{/X}
is quasi-pro-étale by
[
23
, Corollary 18.8.(vii)]
. If
X
is strictly totally disconnected and
U
qcqs, then the natural map
\overline{U}^{/X}\to\pi_{0}(U)\times_{\pi_{0}(X)}X
is an isomorphism as it is qcqs and bijective if
X
is connected. To check (v) we may (by
Lemma

3.25
below) reduce to the case that
X
is strictly totally disconnected. Indeed, in this case
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\overline{U}^{/X}]\cong\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[\pi_{0}(U)\times_{\pi_{0}(X)}X]
is overconvergent, which implies the same for a general
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. This produces the natural map
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]_{\mathrm{oc}}\to\Lambda[\overline{U}^{/X}]
, which then can be checked to be an isomorphism again under the assumption that
X
is strictly totally disconnected. Here, it follows from
Lemma

3.20
as
(-)_{\mathrm{oc}}\cong\pi^{\ast}\pi_{\natural}(-)
.
It follows from the explicit description that the functor
(-)_{\mathrm{oc}}
maps compact objects in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
(resp.
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
) to compact objects in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
(resp.
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
). This implies that the inclusion of overconvergent into solid sheaves preserves compact objects, which implies that the right adjoint
(-)^{\mathrm{oc}}
(for solid or
\omega_{1}
-solid objects) preserves colimits. Now pick a right bounded object
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
. As
X
is
\ell
-bounded can conclude that there exists some
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that for each compact object
N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
, which is supported to the right of
a
and has terms given by
\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
for
U=\varprojlim\limits_{n}U_{n}\to X
with
U_{n}
étale quasi-compact and separated, we have
\Hom(N,M)=0
.
This implies

\Hom(N_{\mathrm{oc}},M)=\Hom(N_{\mathrm{oc}},M^{\mathrm{oc}})=0

for any such
N
by the explict description of
(-)_{\mathrm{oc}}
in (v). This shows that
M^{\mathrm{oc}}
is again right bounded.
∎

The left adjoint
(-)_{\mathrm{oc}}
commutes with base change (while the right adjoint
(-)^{\mathrm{oc}}
does not, even on strictly totally disconnected spaces).

Lemma 3.25
.

Let
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds.

(i)

For
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
