the natural map

(f^{\ast}M)_{\mathrm{oc}}\to f^{\ast}(M_{\mathrm{oc}})

is an isomorphism.

(ii)

If
f
is quasi-pro-étale, then
f_{\ast}(\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}})\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}
and
f_{\ast}(\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}_{\omega_{1}})\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)^{\mathrm{oc}}_{\omega_{1}}

Proof.

By quasi-pro-étale hyperdescent it suffices to treat the case that
X,X^{\prime}
are strictly totally disconnected. Let
\pi_{X}\colon X_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to\pi_{0}(X)_{\mathrm{qproet}},\pi_{X^{\prime}}\colon X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to\pi_{0}(X^{\prime})_{\mathrm{qproet}}
be the morphisms of sites. It suffices to check that the natural map
\pi_{X^{\prime},\natural}f^{\ast}M\to\pi_{0}(f)^{\ast}\pi_{X,\natural}(M)
is an isomorphism for
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)
. Passing to right adjoints, it suffices to see that for
N\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\pi_{0}(X^{\prime}),\Lambda)

f_{\ast}\pi^{\ast}_{X^{\prime}}N\cong\pi^{\ast}_{X}\pi_{0}(f)_{\ast}(N)

via the natural map. This follows from
[
18
, Corollary 5.9]
.
This formula (together with
Lemma

3.17
) imply the second assertion as well if
X
is strictly totally disconnected. The case for general
X
follows by quasi-pro-étale descent (for which
f_{\ast}
satisfies base change by
Lemma

3.17
).
∎

3.5
Nuclear sheaves

In this section we will study nuclear objects in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
and show that they are overconvergent. Critically, we will moreover show in
Proposition

3.35
that the full subcategory of nuclear objects is generated under colimits by right bounded complete objects. In order to achieve this, we need a better understanding of the compact objects of
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}^{\mathrm{oc}}
.

Let us recall from
[
7
, Lecture VIII]
that a morphism
f\colon P\to Q
in a closed symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with compact unit, is called of trace class if it lies in the image of the natural morphism

\pi_{0}((\IHom(P,1)\otimes Q)(\ast))\to\pi_{0}(\Hom(P,Q)).

Here,
\otimes,\IHom,1
refer to the tensor product, internal Hom, unit in
\mathcal{C}
, while
(-)(\ast):=\Hom(1,-)
.
We will use critically the following property of trace class maps:
If
P_{0}\to P_{1}\to\ldots
is a sequential diagram with all morphisms
P_{n}\to P_{n+1}
of trace class, then for any
Q\in\mathcal{C}
the natural map

\varprojlim\limits_{n}(\IHom(P_{n},1)\otimes Q)(\ast))\to\varprojlim\limits_{n}\Hom(P_{n},Q)

is an isomorphism. Indeed, each witness in
(\IHom(P_{n},1)\otimes P_{n+1})(\ast)
for
P_{n}\to P_{n+1}
being trace class yields a factorization

\Hom(P_{n+1},Q)\to(\IHom(P_{n},1)\otimes Q)(\ast)\to\Hom(P_{n},Q).

Following
[
7
, Definition 8.5]
we make the following definition.

Definition 3.26
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond and
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

(a)

M
is called basic nuclear if
M=\varinjlim(M_{0}\to M_{1}\to\ldots)
with all
M_{n}\to M_{n+1}
of trace class.

(b)

M
is called nuclear if for all compact objects
P\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
, the map

(\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(P,\Lambda)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}M)(\ast)\to\Hom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(P,M)

is an equivalence.

We let
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\subseteq\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
be the full subcategory of nuclear objects.

Remark 3.27
.

We warn the reader that the abstract notion of nuclearity from
[
7
, Lecture VIII]
that we employ here is different from the more subtle “geometric nuclearity” in
[
16
, Definition 3.1]
as noted in
[
16
, Warning 3.3]
. In
Lemma

3.30
below we will prove that the abstract notion of nuclearity singles out the
overconvergent
nuclear sheaves from
[
16
, Definition 6.8]
(thereby providing details to
[
16
, Warning 3.3]
).

The basic observation for the overconvergence of the nuclear objects in
\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is the overconvergence of
\IHom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U],\Lambda)
.

Lemma 3.28
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond.

(i)

Each trace class map
P\to Q
in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
factors as
P\to P_{\mathrm{oc}}\to Q
with
P_{\mathrm{oc}}\to Q
of trace class.

(ii)

Each nuclear object in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is overconvergent.

In particular,
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is equivalent to the category
\mathrm{Nuc}(\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}^{\mathrm{oc}})
of nuclear objects in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}^{\mathrm{oc}}
.

Proof.

By
[
7
, Theorem 8.6]
each nuclear object is a colimit of basic nuclear object. By
Lemma

3.24
the subcategory of overconvergent objects is stable under limits and colimits. Hence, it suffices to prove the first assertion to show overconvergence. Let
P\to Q
be a trace class morphism in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
. As
\Lambda
is overconvergent, we can conclude from
Lemma

3.24
that

\IHom(P,\Lambda)\cong\IHom(P_{\mathrm{oc}},\Lambda)

as
(-)_{\mathrm{oc}}
is symmetric monoidal. This implies the desired factorization.

∎

In the strictly totally disconnected case, we can be more explicit and identify nuclear sheaves with nuclear modules over the ring of continuous functions:

Lemma 3.29
.

Let
X
be a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space and let
T
be a profinite set.

(i)

The functors
\pi_{\ast},\pi^{\ast}
induce a symmetric monoidal equivalence
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\cong\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)
.

(ii)

Let
\alpha\colon T_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to\ast_{\mathrm{qproet}}
be the natural morphism of sites. Then
\alpha_{\ast}
induces an equivalence

\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(T_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)\cong\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(C(T,\Lambda)),

where
C(T,\Lambda)\cong\alpha_{\ast}(\Lambda)
is the nuclear
\Lambda
-algebra of continuous functions
T\to\Lambda
.

In particular,
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\cong\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(C(X,\Lambda))
.

We recall that here “nuclear” refers to
Definition

3.26
(and not
[
16
, Definition 3.1]
). However, for
\pi_{0}(X)
both notions agree (by
Lemma

3.29
and
[
16
, Lemma 6.7.(iv)]
or by
[
16
, Remark 3.10]
if
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
).

Proof.

As
\pi^{\ast}
is symmetric monoidal,
\pi^{\ast}
preserves nuclear objects. Moreover, the unit
M\to\pi_{\ast}\pi^{\ast}M
is an isomorphism for any
M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)
by
Lemma

3.19
. Let
N\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
be nuclear. We need to see that the counit
\pi^{\ast}\pi_{\ast}N\to N
is an isomorphism. This follows from
Lemma

3.28
.

Let us proof the second assertion. Set
A:=C(T,\Lambda)
. As
A
is a nuclear
\Lambda
-algebra, e.g., by
2.18.(i)
,
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A)
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda))
, cf.
[
7
, Corollary 8.20]
.
The existence of a left adjoint
\alpha^{\ast}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\ast_{\mathrm{qproet}},\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(T,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
to
\alpha_{\ast}
follows as in the proof of
[
16
, Lemma 6.7.(iv)]
. Similarly, it follows (using
2.18.(i)
) that
\alpha^{\ast}
preserves nuclearity, and that
\alpha_{\ast}\alpha^{\ast}M\to M
is an isomorphism for
M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A)
. It remains to see that
\alpha^{\ast}
is essentially surjective. Here, we a priori have to offer a different proof as in
[
16
, Lemma 6.7.(iv)]
as we use a different notion of nuclearity. However,
[
16
, Remark 3.10]
shows that both notions agree in this case as
T
is profinite. Thus, the assertion follows from
[
16
, Lemma 6.7]
.
∎

We note that
\alpha_{\ast}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(T,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{Mod}_{C(T,\Lambda)}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda)
is in general not an equivalence (if
T
is not finite), even if
\Lambda=\mathbb{F}_{\ell}
. Indeed, for each
t\in T
the morphism
\{t\}\to T
is pro-étale, and thus
\Lambda[\{t\}]\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(T,\Lambda)
is compact. But if
\Lambda
is discrete, then
\alpha_{\ast}(\Lambda[\{t\}])\cong\Lambda
viewed as a
C(T,\Lambda)
-module via evaluation at
t
. If
t\in T
is not an open point, then this implies that
\alpha_{\ast}(\Lambda[\{t\}])
is not a (discrete) finitely presented
C(T,\Lambda)
-module, and hence not compact in
\mathrm{Mod}_{C(T,\Lambda)}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\Lambda)
. Nuclear objects satisfy quasi-pro-étale descent:

Lemma 3.30
.

The functor
X\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is a hypercomplete quasi-pro-étale sheaf on the big quasi-pro-étale site of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds.

Proof.

This is formally implied by
Corollary

3.18
: Pullback of nuclear objects are nuclear (by symmetric monoidality of pullbacks) and if
f_{\bullet}\colon X_{\bullet}\to X
is a quasi-pro-étale hypercover and
M\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
with
f^{\ast}_{n}M
nuclear for each
n\in\Delta
, then for
P\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
compact we have

\displaystyle\Hom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(P,M)=

\displaystyle\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\Delta}\Hom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X_{n},\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(f^{\ast}_{n}P,f^{\ast}_{n}M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\Delta}\Hom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X_{n},\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(\Lambda,\IHom_{X^{\prime}_{n}}(f^{\ast}_{n}P,\Lambda)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}f^{\ast}_{n}M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\Delta}\Hom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X_{n},\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(\Lambda,f^{\ast}_{n}(\IHom_{X}(P,\Lambda)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}M))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Hom_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}}(\Lambda,\IHom(P,\Lambda)\otimes^{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}_{\Lambda}M)

using (in this order) the description of Hom-spaces in limits of categories, nuclearity of all the
f_{n}^{\ast}M
, the fact that
f^{\ast}_{n}
is the restriction for some slice (plus
Proposition

3.16
to control the
\IHom
using that
f^{\ast}_{n}P
is compact), and again the description of Hom-spaces in limits of categories.
∎

The next result analyzes the behavior of completions in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
. More precisely, fix an ideal of definition
I\subseteq\Lambda
. In the following, we adhere to the conventions in
Section

1.2
and in particular implicitly fix a finite set of generators of
I
; morever, we use the terminology “complete” instead of “
I
-adically complete” whenever convenient. Note that a priori completeness in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
and
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
may be different, as
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is not stable under limits in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
. However, the next result shows that in fact, the two notions of completeness coincide:

Lemma 3.31
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond and
M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
.

(i)

The
I
-adic completion
\widehat{M}
of
M
in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
is automatically nuclear.

(ii)

M
is
I
-adically complete in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
if and only if it is so in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

Proof.

We start with (i).
By
Lemma

3.30
and
Lemma

3.17
, specifically the commutation of pullbacks with limits, the claim is quasi-pro-étale local on
X
and hence we may assume that
X
is strictly totally disconnected. By
Lemma

3.19
and
Lemma

3.29
the claim reduces to the case that
X
is a profinite set (realized as a qcqs perfectoid space over some
\Spa(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
with
C
algebraically closed and perfectoid). More precisely, we use that
\pi^{\ast}\colon\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
commutes with limits to see that
\widehat{M}=\pi^{\ast}\pi_{\ast}(\widehat{M})
is the pullback along
\pi^{\ast}
of the completion of
\pi_{\ast}(M)
. Using that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(C(X,\Lambda))
by
Lemma

3.19
, it suffices by
2.18.(i)
to see that
\widehat{M}
is discrete modulo
I
. But
M/I\cong\widehat{M}/I
, and the claim follows.

Now, (ii) is a consequence of (i): Namely, if
\widehat{M}
denotes the
I
-adic completion of
M
in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
then it is also the
I
-adic completion of
M
in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
because
\widehat{M}
is nuclear by (i) and the nuclearization
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
, i.e. the right adjoint to the inclusion, preserves limits.
∎

We now move to a proof of the assertion that the category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is generated by complete objects for any
\ell
-bounded diamond
X
. For this assertion we will use some general properties of dualizable and rigid categories. Let
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
be the
\infty
-category of presentable, stable
\infty
-categories (with colimit preserving functors). We recall that a stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
is dualizable if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is a retract (in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
) of a compactly generated stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
(see
[
6
]
), and this happens if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is generated under colimits by objects of the form
X=\varinjlim_{n}(X_{0}\to X_{1}\to\ldots)
with each morphism
X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
compact. Here, a morphism
X\to Y
in
\mathcal{C}
is called compact if for any filtered diagram
Z_{j},j\in J,
in
\mathcal{C}
with
\varinjlim_{j}Z_{j}\cong 0
, the morphism
\varinjlim_{j}\pi_{0}(\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(Y,Z_{j}))\to\varinjlim_{j}\pi_{0}(\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Z_{j}))
is zero.
A presentably symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}})
is rigid if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable and the compact morphisms are exactly the trace-class morphisms (see
[
6
]
). We note the following abstract property guaranteeing rigidity. Essentially the argument is taken from
[
2
, Satz 3.17]
.
12
12

12

We thank Gregory Andreychev and Peter Scholze for related discussions.

Lemma 3.32
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a compactly generated closed symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category. Assume that
1\in\mathcal{C}
is compact. Then:

(i)

Each trace-class morphism in
\mathcal{C}
is compact.

(ii)

Assume that for each compact object
P\in\mathcal{C}
the internal dual
P^{\vee}=\IHom(P,1)
is nuclear. Then
\mathrm{Nuc}(\mathcal{C})
is rigid. Moreover, the nuclearization functor
(-)^{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{Nuc}(\mathcal{C})
, right adjoint to the inclusion, commutes with colimits, and agrees with the trace functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{tr}}:=\varinjlim_{1\to P\otimes X,\ P\textrm{ compact}}P^{\vee}
.

Proof.

Let
X\to Y
be a trace-class morphism in
\mathcal{C}
with trace-class witness
f\in\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(1,X^{\vee}\otimes Y)
. Writing
Y=\varinjlim_{i}P_{i}
with
P_{i}
compact and using that
1\in\mathcal{C}
is compact, there exists some
P_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
and a factorization
X\to P_{i}\to Y
with
X\to P_{i}
is trace-class. In particular, the morphism
X\to Y
is compact.

For the second assertion, we first discuss the trace functor
X\mapsto X^{{\mathrm{tr}}}
. As before
[
2
, Satz 3.16]
, one can construct a functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
,
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{tr}}
, which is uniquely determined by requiring that (naturally)
\Hom(P,X^{\mathrm{tr}})\cong(P^{\vee}\otimes X)(\ast)
for
P\in\mathcal{C}
compact. Now, the argument of
[
2
, Satz 3.16]
shows that
X^{\mathrm{tr}}\cong\varinjlim_{1\to P\otimes X,\ P\textrm{ compact}}P^{\vee}
for any
X\in\mathcal{C}
. Indeed, by definition of
X^{\mathrm{tr}}
for any compact object
P\in\mathcal{C}
we have a natural isomorphism
\Hom(P,X^{\mathrm{tr}})\cong(P^{\vee}\otimes X)(\ast)
. Writing
P^{\vee}
as a colimit of compact objects
Q
with a morphism
Q\to P^{\vee}
, we can conclude that
\Hom(P,X^{\mathrm{tr}})\cong\varinjlim_{Q\textrm{ compact},Q\to P^{\vee}}(Q\otimes X)(\ast)
. Thus,

\displaystyle X^{\mathrm{tr}}\cong

\displaystyle\varinjlim\limits_{P\textrm{ compact},P\to X^{\mathrm{tr}}}P

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\varinjlim\limits_{P,Q\textrm{ compact},1\ \to Q\otimes X,\ Q\to P^{\vee}}P

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\varinjlim\limits_{P,Q\textrm{ compact},1\ \to Q\otimes X,\ P\to Q^{\vee}}P.

Taking first the colimit over
P
this identifies with
\varinjlim_{Q\textrm{ compact},1\to Q\otimes X}Q^{\vee}
as claimed.

Having checked the formula for the trace functor, the remaining assertions follow. Namely, if
X\in\mathcal{C}
is nuclear, then by definition the natural morphism
X^{\mathrm{tr}}\to X
is an isomorphism. Moreover for any
X\in\mathcal{C}
the object
X^{\mathrm{tr}}
is nuclear by assumption and the above description of
X^{\mathrm{tr}}
(we recall that nuclear objects are stable under all colimits). Clearly, the functor
X\mapsto X^{\mathrm{tr}}
commutes with colimits. Thus the trace functor realizes
\mathrm{Nuc}(\mathcal{C})
as a retract in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
of the compactly generated category
\mathcal{C}
. Hence,
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable. We claim that
(-)^{\mathrm{nuc}}\cong(-)^{\mathrm{tr}}
. For this it suffices to see that for any basic nuclear object
X=\varinjlim_{n}X_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
with each
X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
a trace class morphism between compact objects and any
Y\in\mathcal{C}
, the natural map

\Hom(X,Y^{\mathrm{tr}})\to\Hom(X,Y)

is an isomorphism. This is true as

\Hom(X,Y^{\mathrm{tr}})=\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Hom(X_{n},Y^{\mathrm{tr}})\cong\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(X_{n}^{\vee}\otimes Y)(\ast)\cong\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Hom(X_{n},Y)\cong\Hom(X,Y)

using the definition of
Y^{\mathrm{tr}}
and that each
X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
is a trace-class morphism between compact objects.
Knowing that
\mathrm{Nuc}(\mathrm{C})
is dualizable and that
(-)^{\mathrm{nuc}}
commutes with colimits (as it identifies with
(-)^{\mathrm{tr}}
) formally imply that
\mathrm{Nuc}(\mathcal{C})
is rigid. Namely, by
[
6
]
(and (i)) it suffices to see that each compact morphism
f\colon X\to Y
in
\mathrm{Nuc}(\mathcal{C})
is trace class. As
(-)^{\mathrm{nuc}}
commutes with colimits,
f
is compact in
\mathcal{C}
, which implies that there exists a compact object
P\in\mathcal{C}
and a factorization
X\to P\to Y
of
f
. As
Y
is nuclear, the morphism
P\to Y
is automatically of trace class in
\mathcal{C}
. Let
1\to P^{\vee}\otimes Y
be a witness for
P\to Y
, and let
\IHom^{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,1)
the dual of
X
in
\mathrm{Nuc}(\mathcal{C})
. As
P^{\vee}
is nuclear the natural morphism
P^{\vee}\to\IHom(X,1)
factors over
\IHom^{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,1)
. This implies that the morphism
f\colon X\to Y
is trace class in
\mathrm{Nuc}(\mathcal{C})
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Rigid categories have favourable properties for their module categories.

Lemma 3.33
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\CAlg(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}})
be a presentably stable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category. Assume that
\mathcal{C}
is rigid.

(i)

A
\mathcal{C}
-module
\mathcal{M}
in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
, i.e., an object of
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathcal{C}}
, is dualizable in the symmetric monoidal category
(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathcal{C}},\otimes_{\mathcal{C}})
if and only it is dualizable in
(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}},\otimes)
.

(ii)

Let
F\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathcal{C}}
, and assume that the right adjoint
G\colon\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{M}
commutes with colimits. Then for
A\in\mathcal{C}
and
N\in\mathcal{N}
the natural morphism
13
13

13

This morphism is adjoint to
F(A\otimes G(N))\cong A\otimes F\circ G(N)\to A\otimes N
, with the first isomorphism using
\mathcal{C}
-linearity of
F
.

A\otimes G(N)\to G(A\otimes N),

is an isomorphism. In other words,
G
is
\mathcal{C}
-linear.

(iii)

Any morphism
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{R}
in
\CAlg(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}})
, where the symmetric monoidal unit
1_{\mathcal{R}}\in\mathcal{R}
is compact, automatically has a colimit-preserving right adjoint
G
, i.e., is strongly continuous.

(iv)

If
A\in\CAlg(\mathcal{C})
, then
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
is rigid.

Proof.

Statement (i) is proven in
[
10
, Proposition 9.4.4]
(for an equivalent characterization of rigid categories). Statement (ii) is
[
10
, Lemma 9.3.6]
. Let us prove (iii). Let
Z_{i}\in\mathcal{R},i\in I
, be a collection of objects. We have to see that for any
X\in\mathcal{C}
the natural morphism

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}G(Z_{i}))\to\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,G(\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}Z_{i}))

is an isomorphism. We may assume that
X=\varinjlim_{n}X_{n}
is basic nuclear with
X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
of trace class. Then

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}G(Z_{i}))\cong\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(X_{n}^{\vee}\otimes\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}G(Z_{i}))(\ast)\cong\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}(X^{\vee}_{n}\otimes G(Z_{i}))(\ast)\cong\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}\Hom(X_{n},G(Z_{i}))

using that
X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
is of trace class and
1\in\mathcal{C}
compact (as
\mathrm{Id}_{1}
is of trace class, hence compact). On the other hand,

\Hom(X,G(\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}Z_{i}))\cong\Hom(F(X),\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}Z_{i})\cong\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(F(X_{n})^{\vee}\otimes\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}Z_{i})\cong\varprojlim\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}\Hom(F(X_{n}),Z_{i})

using that
F(X_{n})\to F(X_{n+1})
is of trace class and
1\in\mathcal{R}
compact. This implies the assertion as
F
is left adjoint to
G
.

Assume that
A\in\CAlg(\mathcal{C})
. As the right adjoint of the functor
A\otimes(-)\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
, i.e., the forgetful functor, preserves colimits, the functor
A\otimes(-)
preserves compact morphisms. As
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable, this implies that
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
is generated by objects, which are colimits along compact morphisms. This implies that
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable. In fact,
\mathcal{C}
is generated by colimits along trace-class maps, which by symmetric monoidality of the functor
A\otimes(-)
implies the same for
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
, i.e., rigidity of
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
.
∎

Example 3.34
.

Lemma

3.32
and
2.18.(i)
imply that for any adic analytic ring
(A,A^{+})
in the sense of
Definition

2.1
the category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A,A^{+})
is rigid. We note that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A,A^{+})
does not depend on
A^{+}
.

We now apply these general assertions on rigid categories to nuclear sheaves on
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds in order to show that they are generated under colimits by complete objects. In fact, we have:

Proposition 3.35
.

Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond. Then
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
is rigid and generated by complete objects which are right-bounded in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
. Moreover, the nuclearization functor

\displaystyle(-)^{\mathrm{nuc}}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda),

right adjoint to the inclusion, preserves all small colimits.

Proof.

By
Lemma

3.32
it suffices to see that for each compact object
P\in\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
the object
P^{\vee}
is right-bounded and nuclear. In fact, we may assume that
P=\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[U]
for some basic quasi-pro-étale
U\to X
. We note that
P^{\vee}
is right-bounded, complete, overconvergent and discrete modulo some ideal of definition
I\subseteq\Lambda
. We claim that these properties imply nuclearity. By
Lemma

3.31
and
Lemma

3.30
we can reduce this claim to the case that
X
is a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space (because the pullback will again be right-bounded, complete, overconvergent and discrete mod
I
). By
Lemma

3.29
the assertion then reduces to
Lemma

2.18
.
∎

Remark 3.36
.

A convenient source for trace-class maps in
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(X,\Lambda)
is the following. Let
X
be an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond and
f\colon U\to V,g\colon V\to X
étale, quasi-compact and separated maps. Assume that
\Lambda
is discrete (but potentially derived). If
f\colon U\to V
extends to a map
h\colon\overline{U}^{/X}\to V
, then
\Lambda[U]\to\Lambda[V]
is trace class in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(X,\Lambda)_{\omega_{1}}
.

3.6
\mathcal{C}
-valued nuclear sheaves

In order to prove strong descent results for
\mathcal{O}^{+}
-modules on perfectoid spaces, we need a slightly exotic version of nuclear
\Lambda
-modules, where we introduce an additional “algebraic” solid structure. In fact, the following definitions and results work more generally for certain
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear categories
\mathcal{C}
.

We continue with the notation from
Section

3.4
, in particular
\Lambda
is an adic and profinite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra.

Remark 3.37
.

The category
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}
of presentable
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear categories has some favourable properties due to the rigidity of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
as a presentably, symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category (see
Lemmas

3.33
and
3.34
).

The
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-modules that we will interested in have particular properties. In the following we use the short hand notation “complete” instead of “
I
-adically complete for some ideal of definition
I\subseteq\Lambda
”.

Definition 3.38
.

We say that a
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear presentable category
\mathcal{C}
is
nicely generated
if there is a small family of objects
(P_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{C}
satisfying the following properties:

(a)

For every bounded complete
M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
and all
i\in I
the object
M\otimes P_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
is complete.

(b)

For every
i\in I
the functor
\Hom^{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}(P_{i},-)\colon\mathcal{C}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
preserves all small colimits.

(c)

The family of functors
\Hom^{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}(P_{i},-)
from (ii) is conservative.

Here,
\Hom^{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}(X,-)
for
X\in\mathcal{C}
is the functor right adjoint to the functor
(-)\otimes_{\Lambda}X\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)\to\mathcal{C},M\mapsto M\otimes_{\Lambda}X
. In particular,

\Hom_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}(\Lambda,\Hom^{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}(X,Y))\cong\Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)

(3.38.1)

for
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
.

Lemma 3.39
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a nicely generated
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear presentable category.

(i)

The category
\mathcal{C}
is compactly generated, and dualizable as an object in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}
.

(ii)

For every compact
P\in\mathcal{C}
the functor
\Hom^{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}(P,-)\colon\mathcal{C}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
is
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear.

Proof.

As
\Lambda\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
is compact, each
P_{i}
from
Definition

3.38
is compact, hence
\mathcal{C}
is compactly generated. By
[
15
, Proposition D.7.2.3]
the category
\mathcal{C}
is therefore dualizable in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{Sp}}
, and hence in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}
by
Remark

3.37
. If
P\in\mathcal{C}
is compact, then the
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear functor
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)\to\mathcal{C},\ A\mapsto A\otimes P
has the colimit-preserving right adjoint
\Hom^{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}(P,-)\colon\mathcal{C}\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
, which is therefore
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear by
Remark

3.37
.
∎

Recall that in
Definition

3.26
we have introduced the
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
for an
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond
X
. Using this category, we now give the following definition.

Definition 3.40
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a nicely generated
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear presentable category. Then for every
\ell
-bounded spatial diamond
X
we denote

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\mathcal{C}):=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\otimes_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}\mathcal{C}

and call it the
category of
\mathcal{C}
-valued nuclear (or nuclear overconvergent) sheaves on
X
.

Proposition 3.41
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a nicely generated
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear presentable category. Then:

(i)

The assignment
X\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\mathcal{C})
defines a hypercomplete sheaf on the big quasi-pro-étale site of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds.

(ii)

For every
\ell
-bounded diamond
X
the category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\mathcal{C})
is generated under colimits by
\pi
-adically complete objects.

(iii)

If
X
is a strictly totally disconnected space then
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\mathcal{C})
naturally identifies with the category of
C(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)
-modules in
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Using
[
13
, Theorem 4.8.4.6]
claim (iii) follows from the fact that

\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\cong\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(C(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda))\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{C(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda)}(\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda))

if
X
is strictly totally disconnected (see
Lemma

3.29
and its proof).

Let us prove claim (i) and take a quasi-pro-étale hypercover
X_{\bullet}\to X
of
\ell
-bounded spatial diamonds. We need to see that the natural functor

F\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\mathcal{C})\to\varprojlim_{n\in\Delta}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X_{n},\mathcal{C})

is an equivalence. By
Lemma

3.30
this holds if
\mathcal{C}=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
, and then in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}
, i.e., all transition functors are
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-linear (as they are induced by pullback functors for
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,\Lambda)
). Therefore the claim follows by dualizability of
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}
as established in
Lemma

3.39
. Indeed, dualizability implies that
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}-
commutes with limits in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)}
.

It remains to prove (ii), so let
X
be a given
\ell
-bounded diamond. Then
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\mathcal{C})
is generated under colimits by objects
\mathcal{M}\boxtimes P
, where
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
and
P\in\mathcal{C}
is compact (and hence also complete by our assumption that
\mathcal{C}
is nicely generated). By
Proposition

3.35
we may assume that
M
is complete, and additionally bounded above.

It is therefore enough to show that each of these
\mathcal{M}\boxtimes P
is complete. By (i) we can check this after pullback to any quasi-pro-étale hypercover (using that limits of complete objects are again complete), so we can assume that
X
is strictly totally disconnected. Then the claim follows from (iii) and the fact that complete objects in
\mathcal{C}
are stable under the action of complete objects in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
by assumption. More precisely, completeness of
M\boxtimes P
can be checked after applying the forgetful functor to
\mathcal{C}
, and there
M\boxtimes P
is given by
|M|\otimes P
with
|M|\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
the underlying object of
M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)\cong\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(C(\pi_{0}(X),\Lambda))
and
\otimes
the given
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\Lambda)
-action on
\mathcal{C}
.
∎

4
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}^{a}(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+})
for perfectoid spaces

In this section we will prove the first part of
Theorem

1.1
, i.e.,
v
-descent of almost
\mathcal{O}^{+}
-sheaves on perfectoid spaces for our modified version of
+
-modules from
Definition

2.4
.

4.1
Definition of
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}^{a}(A^{+})

As in
[
17
, Definition 3.1.1]
we denote by
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
the category of affinoid perfectoid spaces over
\mathrm{Spf}\,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. We note that each object
(A,A^{+})\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
is classical and static and hence we will often not distinguish between
(A,A^{+})
and the classical Tate-Huber pair
(A(\ast),A^{+}(\ast))
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
(A,A^{+})\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
, and let
A^{\circ\circ}\subseteq A^{+}
be the ideal of topologically nilpotent elements. Then
A^{+}/A^{\circ\circ}
is an idempotent algebra in the classical derived category
\D(A^{+})=\D(A^{+}(\ast))
of
A^{+}
-modules.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
A^{\circ\circ}
is a filtered colimit of principal ideals generated by non-zero divisors, and that
A^{\circ\circ}\cdot A^{\circ\circ}=A^{\circ\circ}
.
∎

Definition 4.2
.

Let
(A,A^{+})\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
. Then we define
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}^{a}(A^{+}):=\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}((A^{+},A^{+}))\otimes_{\D(A^{+})}\D^{a}(A^{+})
, where
\D(A^{+})\to\D^{a}(A^{+})
is the open immersion defined by the idempotent algebra
A^{+}/A^{\circ\circ}
from
Lemma

4.1
.

Remark 4.3
.

By
Lemma

2.35
we have
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}^{a}(A^{+})=\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}((A^{+},A^{+}))/\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{+}/A^{\circ\circ}}(\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}((A^{+},A^{+})))
.

Remark 4.4
.

We note that
A^{+}\cong A^{\circ}
in
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
as
A^{\circ\circ}\subseteq A^{+}
. Thus,
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}((A^{\circ},A^{+})),\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}((A^{+},A^{+})
become isomorphic after
(-)\otimes_{\D(A)}\D^{a}(A)
. However, in general
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
and
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{\circ})
are different.

Remark 4.5
.

Assume that
\pi\in A\cap A^{+}
is a pseudo-uniformizer. Then
\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{+}/\pi}(\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}))\cong\D^{a}_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}/\pi)
is naturally equivalent to the category defined in
[
17
, Definition 3.1.2]
because
\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{+}/\pi}(\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}))\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{+}/\pi}(\D_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}))\otimes_{\D(A^{+})}\D^{a}(A^{+})\cong\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)
using
Lemma

2.10
in the first isomorphism.

The following special property is the basic reason why (almost)
v
-descent results for
+
-modules on perfectoid spaces are possible while they fail drastically for non-perfectoid spaces.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

be a cartesian diagram in
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
. Write
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
,
X^{\prime}=\Spa(A^{\prime},A^{\prime+})
,
Y=\Spa(B,B^{+})
and
Y^{\prime}=\Spa(B^{\prime},B^{\prime+})
. Then the natural morphism of analytic rings

\displaystyle(A^{\prime+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{a}\otimes_{(A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{a}}(B^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{a}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}(B^{\prime+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}^{a}.

is an isomorphism.

Here, we view
(A^{+})^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
etc. as analytic rings over the almost setup
(A^{+},A^{\circ\circ})
following
[
17
, Section 2.3]
. More concretely, the assertion means that the natural functor
\D((B^{\prime})^{+}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})\to\D((A^{\prime+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\otimes_{A^{+}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}B^{+}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})
becomes an equivalence after
(-)\otimes_{\D(A^{+})}\D^{a}(A^{+})
.

Proof.

Since
B^{\prime+}
is the (completed) integral closure of the image of
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime+}\otimes_{A^{+}}B^{+})
in
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}B)
, by
Lemma

2.24
it is enough to show that the map

\displaystyle A^{\prime+a}\hat{\otimes}_{A^{+a}}B^{+a}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}B^{\prime+a}

is an isomorphism of almost rings. But both sides of this claimed isomorphism are
\pi
-adically complete for any pseudouniformizer
\pi
of
A
, hence the claim can be checked modulo
\pi
, where it follows from
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.6]
.
∎

As a corollary from the proof we see that the derived (completed) tensor product
A^{\prime+a}\otimes_{A^{+a}}B^{+a}
is in fact static, i.e., concentrated in degre
0
. This statement also holds before passing to the almost category by reduction to the case of perfect rings.

4.2
Descent on totally disconnected spaces

In this section, we prove the first part of
Theorem

1.1
, i.e., the existence of the
v
-sheaf
X\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
on perfectoid spaces. More precisely, in this section we prove the following theorem.

Theorem 4.7
.

There is a unique hypercomplete v-sheaf of categories

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Perfd})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{C}at,\qquad X\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})

such that for every
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
which admits a quasi-pro-étale map to a totally disconnected space we have

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}).

We recall that a qcqs perfectoid space
X
is totally disconnected if each connected component of
X
has the form
\Spa(K,K^{+})
for some perfectoid field
K
with open and bounded valuation subring
K^{+}\subseteq K
(
[
23
, Lemma 7.3]
), in which case
X=\Spa(R,R^{+})
is necessarily affinoid, and for any pseudo-uniformizer
\pi\in R
we have
(|X|,\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X}/\pi)\cong\Spec(R^{+}/\pi)
as locally ringed spaces (
[
17
, Lemma 3.6.1]
).

Totally disconnected perfectoid spaces enjoy the following surprising flatness property (
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.7]
).

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be a totally disconnected perfectoid space, and
\pi\in A
a uniformizer. Let
A^{\prime}
be a
\pi
-torsion free
A^{+}
-algebra of finite type. Then for every connected component
x=\Spec((A^{+}/\pi)_{x})
of
\Spec(A^{+}/\pi)
the map
(A^{+}/\pi)_{x}\to A^{\prime}\otimes_{A^{+}}(A^{+}/\pi)_{x}
is flat and finitely presented.

Proof.

This is
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.7]
.
∎

This result strengthens
[
23
, Proposition 7.23]
and is critical for
Theorem

1.2
as it implies descent for
\mathcal{O}^{+}/\pi
-modules for a
v
-cover
f\colon Y\to X
with
X
totally disconnected (
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.8]
). The proof of loc. cit. uses critically that the (pre)sheaf
\Spa(R,R^{+})\mapsto R^{+}/\pi
on affinoid perfectoid spaces sends cofiltered inverse limits to filtered colimits, roughly to reduce to a single connected component of
X
where
Lemma

4.8
applies. This argument does not work for the sheaf
\mathcal{O}^{+}
. Instead, we roughly spread out the flatness mod
\pi
on a connected component (provided by
Lemma

4.8
) to some neighborhood, at least up to replacing
Y
by some disjoint union of some finite open cover. For the precise assertion below, we denote by
Y^{\mathrm{wl}}\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
the w-localization of any
Y\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
, as defined in
[
23
, Proposition 7.12]
.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
Y=\Spa(B,B^{+})\to X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be a surjective map of totally disconnected spaces in
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
. Then the map
Y^{\mathrm{wl}}\to X
is
+
-weakly adically descendable of index
\leq 4
, i.e., if
Y^{\mathrm{wl}}=\Spa(B_{\mathrm{wl}},B_{\mathrm{wl}}^{+})
the map
(A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
is weakly adically descendable of index
\leq 4
in the sense of
Definition

2.32
.

Proof.

We need to show that the map
A^{+}\to B_{\mathrm{wl}}^{+}
of adic rings is an
\omega_{1}
-filtered colimit of adically descendable maps of index
\leq 4
. Let
I
be the category of factorizations
Y^{\mathrm{wl}}\to Y_{i}\to Y
, where
Y_{i}
is a finite disjoint union of qcqs open subsets of
Y
. Then by
[
23
, Lemma 7.13]

I
is cofiltered and
Y^{\mathrm{wl}}=\varprojlim_{i\in I}Y_{i}
. We denote
Y_{i}=\Spa(B_{i},B^{+}_{i})
, so that
B_{\mathrm{wl}}^{+}
is the completed filtered colimit of the
B^{+}_{i}
.

In the following discussion, all rings and modules will be discrete and static unless stated otherwise. For some fixed pseudouniformizer
\pi\in A
we let
J_{\pi}
be the following category: An object of
J_{\pi}
is a factorization
A^{+}\to A_{j}\to B_{\mathrm{wl}}^{+}
of
A^{+}
-algebras such that the map
A^{+}\to A_{j}
is a finitely presented map of classical rings which is flat modulo
\pi
(here we take the
underived
quotient
A_{j}/\pi
!) and such that the map
A_{j}\to B_{\mathrm{wl}}^{+}
factors over some
B^{+}_{i}
. A morphism in
J_{\pi}
is a morphism
\alpha\colon A_{j}\to A_{j^{\prime}}
over
A^{+}
such that there is some
i\in I
with the property that both
A_{j}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
and
A_{j^{\prime}}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
factor over
B^{+}_{i}
and these factorizations are compatible with
\alpha
. We now claim that
J_{\pi}
has the following crucial property:

(a)

Let
A_{j}\in J_{\pi}
. Pick some
i
such that
A_{j}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
factors over
B^{+}_{i}
and some
a\in A_{j}
which is sent to
0
via
A_{j}\to B^{+}_{i}
. Then there is some morphism
\alpha\colon A_{j}\to A_{j^{\prime}}
in
J_{\pi}
such that
\alpha(a)=0
.

We now prove this claim, so fix
A_{j}
,
i
and
a
as in the claim. Let
A^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq B^{+}_{i}
be the image of
A_{j}
and fix some connected component
x\in\pi_{0}(X)
. Equivalently
x
is a connected component of
\Spec A^{+}/\pi
, i.e. of the form
x=\Spec(A^{+}/\pi)_{x}
, as
X
is totally disconnected. For any
A^{+}
-algebra
A^{\prime}
we denote
(A^{\prime}/\pi)_{x}:=A^{\prime}\otimes_{A^{+}}(A^{+}/\pi)_{x}
. Then by
Lemma

4.8
the map
(A^{+}/\pi)_{x}\to(A^{\prime}_{j}/\pi)_{x}
is flat and finitely presented for
A^{\prime}_{j}
as above and in particular the map
\beta_{x,\pi}\colon(A_{j}/\pi)_{x}\to(A^{\prime}_{j}/\pi)_{x}
is finitely presented (
[
28
, Tag 00F4]
). Pick some generators
\overline{a}_{1,x},\dots,\overline{a}_{n,x}
of
\ker(\beta_{x,\pi})
. They automatically extend over some (pullback of some) clopen neighbourhood of
x
in
\Spec A^{+}/\pi
and hence to all of
A_{j}/\pi
by extending them by
0
on the (pullback of the) clopen complement of that neighbourhood. Moreover, we can guarantee that these extensions
\overline{a}_{1},\dots,\overline{a}_{n}\in A_{j}/\pi
lie in the kernel of the map
A_{j}/\pi\to A^{\prime}_{j}/\pi
by shrinking the clopen neighbourhood of
x
if necessary. Since the map
\beta\colon A_{j}\to A^{\prime}_{j}
is surjective, we can find lifts
a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\in A_{j}
of
\overline{a}_{1},\dots,\overline{a}_{n}
which lie in the kernel of
\beta
. We can assume that
a
is one of the
a_{k}
’s as by assumption
a
maps to
0
in
A_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq B_{i}^{+}
. Now
denote

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{j,x}:=A_{j}/(a_{1},\dots,a_{n}).

Then
A^{\prime}_{j,x}
is a finitely presented
A^{+}
-algebra with a map to
A^{\prime}_{j}
such that
(A^{\prime}_{j,x}/\pi)_{x}=(A^{\prime}_{j}/\pi)_{x}
and such that
a=0
in
A^{\prime}_{j,x}
. By
[
28
, Tag 00RC]
the locus
U\subseteq\Spec A^{\prime}_{j,x}/\pi
which is flat over
A^{+}/\pi
is an open subset and by construction we have
\Spec(A^{\prime}_{j,x}/\pi)_{x}\subseteq U
. We can thus find elements
\overline{f}_{1},\dots\overline{f}_{m}\in A^{\prime}_{j,x}/\pi
such that each localization
(A^{\prime}_{j,x}/\pi)_{\overline{f}_{k}}
is flat over
A^{+}/\pi
and such that the associated schemes cover
\Spec(A^{\prime}_{j,x}/\pi)_{x}
. By the proof of
[
17
, Lemma 3.6.1]
the elements
\overline{f}_{1},\dots,\overline{f}_{m}
, more precisely their images in
B_{i}^{+}/\pi
, induce qcqs open, even rational, subsets
V_{1,x},\dots V_{m,x}\subseteq Y_{i}
such that
\overline{f}_{k}
is a unit in
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y_{i}}(V_{k,x})
for
k=1,\ldots,m
(here we use that
Y
and hence
Y_{i}
is totally disconnected in order to apply
[
17
, Lemma 3.6.1]
). Pick any lifts
f_{1},\dots,f_{m}\in A^{\prime}_{j,x}
and denote

\displaystyle A_{j,x}:=\prod_{k=1}^{m}(A^{\prime}_{j,x})_{f_{k}},\qquad Y_{i,x}:=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{m}V_{k,x}.

Writing
Y_{i,x}=\Spa(B_{i,x},B^{+}_{i,x})
we get a map
A_{j,x}\to B^{+}_{i,x}
of
A^{+}
-algebras which is compatible with the natural maps
A_{j}\to A_{j,x}
and
B^{+}_{i}\to B^{+}_{i,x}
. Moreover, by construction the subsets
Y_{k,x}
cover the fiber of
Y_{i}
over
x
(because the distinguished opens
D(\overline{f}_{1}),\ldots,D(\overline{f}_{m})
cover
\Spec(A^{\prime}_{j,x}/\pi)_{x})
, and hence their pullbacks cover
\Spec(B^{+}_{i}/\pi)
). Since
Y_{i}
is qcqs, we can find finitely many connected components
x_{1},\dots,x_{\ell}
of
X
such that the map
Y_{i^{\prime}}:=Y_{i,x_{1}}\mathbin{\sqcup}\dots\mathbin{\sqcup}Y_{i,x_{\ell}}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}Y_{i}
is a cover, so that
Y_{i^{\prime}}\in I
. Let

\displaystyle A_{j^{\prime}}:=\prod_{k=1}^{\ell}A_{j,x_{k}}.

Then
A_{j^{\prime}}\in J_{\pi}
and the natural map
\alpha\colon A_{j}\to A_{j^{\prime}}
satisfies the claim (a). This proves (a).

We note that we did not use that
A_{j}/\pi
is flat over
A^{+}/\pi
in the proof, only flatness of
A^{\prime}_{j}/\pi
, which was supplied by
Lemma

4.8
as the
A^{\prime}_{j}
was a finitely generated,
\pi
-torsion free
A^{+}
-algebra. Replacing in the argument
\pi
by
\pi^{n},n\geq 1,
we see that we can even guarantee that
A_{j^{\prime}}/\pi^{n}
is flat over
A^{+}/\pi^{n}
.

From now on, we work with derived condensed rings again. With (a) at hand, we can now prove the following claim:

(b)

The category
J_{\pi}
is filtered and
B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
is the (derived)
\pi
-adically completed colimit of the system
(\hat{A}_{j})_{j\in J_{\pi}}
, where
\hat{A}_{j}
denotes the (derived)
\pi
-adic completion of
A_{j}
.

Let us first show that
J_{\pi}
is filtered. Clearly,
J_{\pi}
is non-empty as
A^{+}\in J_{\pi}
. Given
A_{j},A_{j^{\prime}}\in J_{\pi}
, pick any
i\in I
such that both
A_{j}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
and
A_{j^{\prime}}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
factor over
B^{+}_{i}
. Then the static, non-condensed tensor product
A_{j^{\prime\prime}}:=A_{j}\otimes_{A^{+}}A_{j^{\prime}}
lies in
J_{\pi}
and the map
A_{j^{\prime\prime}}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
factors over
B^{+}_{i}
. In particular the natural maps
A_{j}\to A_{j^{\prime\prime}}
and
A_{j^{\prime}}\to A_{j^{\prime\prime}}
lie in
J_{\pi}
. Now suppose we have two maps
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\colon A_{j}\to A_{j^{\prime}}
in
J_{\pi}
. By repeatedly applying (a) to the images of polynomial generators of
A_{j}
under
\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}
we can construct some
A_{j^{\prime\prime}}\in J_{\pi}
with a map
A_{j^{\prime}}\to A_{j^{\prime\prime}}
such that
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
become equal on
A_{j^{\prime\prime}}
. This proves that
J_{\pi}
is indeed filtered.

Now let
B^{\prime}
be the (derived)
\pi
-adic completion of
\varinjlim_{j\in J_{\pi}}\hat{A}_{j}
. There is a natural map
B^{\prime}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
of
\pi
-adic rings which we need to show to be an isomorphism. This can be checked modulo
\pi
, so we need so show that the natural map

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{j}\hat{A}_{j}/\pi=\varinjlim_{j}A_{j}/\pi\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}/\pi=\varinjlim_{i}B^{+}_{i}/\pi

is an isomorphism of discrete rings (here the tensor product
A_{j}/\pi
is a priori derived!). For this it is enough to show the stronger claim that the natural map
\varinjlim_{j}A_{j}\to\varinjlim_{i}B^{+}_{i}
of classical, non-condensed rings is an isomorphism of static discrete rings. This map is clearly surjective, because every
b\in B^{+}_{i}
is hit by some
A_{j}\to B^{+}_{i}
, e.g., for
A_{j}=A^{+}[x]
and
x\mapsto b
. The map is injective by claim (a). This proves the desired isomorphism and thus also claim (b).

We have now shown that the map
A^{+}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
is the
\pi
-completed filtered colimit of
\pi
-complete finitely presented
A^{+}
-algebras whose underived reduction modulo
\pi
(i.e.
\pi_{0}(A_{j}/\pi)
) is flat over
A^{+}/\pi
. Since we only talk about underived reductions modulo
\pi
, we cannot immediately conclude the desired descendability statement: Since we have no control over the
\pi
-torsion of
A_{j}\in J_{\pi}
, we cannot deduce from the flatness of
\pi_{0}(A_{j}/\pi)
that also
\pi_{0}(A_{j}/\pi^{n})
is flat. We therefore need the following additional argument.

From now on we denote
J_{n}:=J_{\pi^{n}}
for some fixed pseudouniformizer
\pi\in A
. Then
J_{n+1}\subseteq J_{n}
is a full subcategory for every
n\geq 1
and as we noted after the proof of (a) this subcategory is even cofinal. Now let
J
be the following category: The objects of
J
are the functors
j\colon\mathbb{N}\to J_{1}
such that
j(n)\in J_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
. For
j,j^{\prime}\in J
we define

\displaystyle\Hom_{J}(j,j^{\prime})=\varinjlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Hom({j}|_{\mathbb{N}_{\geq n}},{j^{\prime}}|_{\mathbb{N}_{\geq n}}).

To every
j\in J
we associate the
A^{+}
-algebra
A_{j}:=\varinjlim_{n}A_{j(n)}
. It follows from (a) that
J
is
\omega_{1}
-filtered
14
14

14

Given any countable diagram
j_{k},k\in K,
in
J
, we can write the category
K
as a filtered union of finite subgraphs
K_{n},n\in\mathbb{N},
and set
j^{\prime}(n)\in J_{n}
as an object with a map from each
j_{k}(l),k\in K_{n}
and
l\leq n
such that these morphisms to
j^{\prime}(n)
equalize all morphisms
j_{k}(l)\to j_{k^{\prime}}(l)
induced by a morphism in
K_{n}
. Inductively, we can even construct compatible morphisms
j^{\prime}(n)\to j^{\prime}(n+1)
, and set
j_{k}\to j^{\prime}
for
k\in K_{n}
as the morphism, which for
m\geq n
is the chosen morphism
j_{k}(m)\to j^{\prime}(m)
.
and it follows as in (b) that
B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}
is the
\pi
-completed colimit of
(\hat{A}_{j})_{j\in J}
. Thus, to finish the proof it is now enough to show the following claim:

(c)

For every
j\in J
the map
(A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(\hat{A}_{j})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
is adically descendable of index
\leq 4
.

To prove (c), fix
j\in J
and
n\geq 1
. We need to show that the map
(A^{+}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(A_{j}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
is descendable of index
\leq 4
. This map is the filtered colimit of the maps
(A^{+}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(A_{j(k)}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
, so by
[
17
, Proposition 2.7.2]
it is enough to show that for
k\gg 0
the map
(A^{+}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(A_{j(k)}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
is descendable of index
\leq 2
. We claim that this works for
k\geq n
. Indeed, we have the factorization

\displaystyle\alpha\colon(A^{+}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(A_{j(k)}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to\pi_{0}(A_{j(k)}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}},

and by definition of
J_{n}
the composed map
\alpha
is flat and finitely presented. The map
\alpha
is even surjective on spectra, and thus an fppf cover, because its composition with
\pi_{0}(A_{j(k)}/\pi^{n})\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}/\pi^{n}
is
A^{+}/\pi^{n}\to B^{+}_{\mathrm{wl}}/\pi^{n}
, which is surjective on spectra (as
Y^{\mathrm{wl}}\to X
is a
v
-cover of totally disconnected spaces). Thus by
[
17
, Lemma 2.10.6]
the map
\alpha
is descendable of index
\leq 2
, so by
[
17
, Lemma 2.6.10.(ii)]
the same is true for the first map
(A^{+}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(A_{j(k)}/\pi^{n})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
, as desired.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

4.7
.

It is sufficient to construct
X\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
for
X\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
as it will formally extend via analytic descent.
We define the presheaf
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\mapsto F(X):=\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}^{a}(A^{+})
on
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
.
We first observe that for every v-cover
f\colon Y=\Spa(B,B^{+})\to X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
of totally disconnected spaces, the presheaf
F
satisfies descent along
f
. Indeed, by
Proposition

4.9
and
Lemma

4.6
the presheaf
F
satisfies descent along the map
Y^{\mathrm{wl}}\to X
, even after any base change
X^{\prime}\to X
with
X^{\prime}
any affinoid, not necessarily totally disconnected, perfectoid space.
15
15

15

More precisely, we as well use that descent of
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(-)
for
(A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(B^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
implies descent of
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}^{a}(-)
by embedding the almost category (via
j_{!}
) into
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(-)
and using
Remark

2.36
to see that this embedding is compatible with pullback.
Hence, by
[
12
, Lemma 3.1.2.(3)]
the same is true for
f\colon Y\to X
. Using
Lemma

4.6
we can argue as in
[
17
, Proposition 3.1.9]
to show that there is a (necessarily unique) quasi-pro-étale sheaf
X\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
on
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
which has the desired form on those spaces which are quasi-pro-étale over some totally disconnected space. We recall the argument for the convenience of the reader: first, the presheaf
F
defines a quasi-pro-étale sheaf on the pro-étale site
16
16

16

We note that a qcqs perfectoid space
Y
with a quasi-pro-étale map
Y\to X
to a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space
X
is itself again strictly totally disconnected. This need not be true if
X=\Spa(K,K^{+})
is connected and totally disconnected as the valuation ring
K^{+}
need not be henselian.
of
strictly
totally disconnected perfectoid spaces, i.e., those totally disconnected spaces whose connected components are of the form
\Spa(C,C^{+})
with
C
algebraically closed and
C^{+}\subseteq C
an open and bounded valuation subring. Formally, this quasi-pro-étale sheaf extends to the quasi-pro-étale sheaf
X\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
on
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
. Assume that
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
is affinoid perfectoid with a quasi-pro-étale map
X\to Z
to a totally disconnected space
Z
. If
Y\to Z
is a quasi-pro-étale cover with
Y
strictly totally disconnected, then
Y\to Z
satisfies universal descent for
F
as we have seen above. In particular,
F
satisfies descent for the cover
Y^{\prime}:=Y\times_{Z}X\to X
. As the terms of the Čech nerve for
Y^{\prime}\to X
are strictly totally disconnected, we can therefore conclude that
F(X)=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
as claimed.

It remains to show that the sheaf
X\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
satisfies v-hyperdescent. This can be done in the same way as in the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 3.1.27]
: One first shows the analog of
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.23]
for adic coefficients, where the two crucial inputs are
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.22]
(which can be replaced by
Lemma

4.10
) and the finite Tor dimension (which can be lifted to the adic level by
Corollary

2.14
). Then the adic version of
[
17
, Corollary 3.1.24]
is a formal corollary and consequently we deduce the adic version of
[
17
, Proposition 3.1.25]
; here the only non-formal input is the fact that
+
-modules, i.e.,
\D^{+}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
for
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
, descend along v-covers of totally disconnected spaces, as shown above. In other words, we have now shown that
X\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
is a v-sheaf. To get v-hyperdescent it remains to prove the adic version of
[
17
, Proposition 3.1.26]
. But here the only non-formal input is the finite Tor dimension, which can again be deduced from the mod-
\pi
version via
Corollary

2.14
.
∎

The following result was used in the proof of
Theorem

4.7
above. It is an interesting statement on its own, so we extract it here:

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be an étale cover in
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
. Then
+
-modules descend along
j
, i.e., the functor
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
is an étale sheaf on
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
.

Proof.

After inverting a pseudo-uniformizer
\pi\in A
, i.e., for the functor
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\mapsto\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}))=\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}))
this follows from
Theorem

2.38
. Using the fiber sequence
A^{+}\to A\to A/A^{+}=\varinjlim\limits_{n}A^{+}/\pi^{n}
reduces therefore to the descent for the functor
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\mapsto\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{+}/\pi^{n}}(\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}))=\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{+}/\pi^{n}}(\D^{a}_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}))
. Moreover, using induction and the fiber sequence
A^{+}/\pi^{n-1}\to A^{+}/\pi^{n}\to A^{+}/\pi
reduces to the case
n=1
. As
\pi
was arbitrary, we may even assume that
p|\pi
, and then (by tilting) that
X=\Spa(A,A^{+}),Y=\Spa(B,B^{+})
are perfectoid spaces of characteristic
p
. As
f\colon Y\to X
is étale, there exists by
[
23
, Proposition 6.4.(iv)]
some étale morphism
f_{0}\colon Y_{0}=\Spa(B_{0},B_{0}^{+}\to X_{0}=\Spa(A_{0},A_{0}^{+})
of affinoid perfectoid spaces, which are weakly of perfectly finite type over
\Spa(\mathbb{F}_{p}((\pi^{1/p^{\infty}})))
(in the sense of
[
17
, Definition 3.1.13.]
), and morphisms
g\colon X\to X_{0},Y\to Y_{0}
such that
Y\cong Y_{0}\times_{X_{0}}X
. By
[
17
, Theorem 3.1.17]
the natural functor

\D^{a}_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A_{0}^{+}/\pi)\to\varprojlim\limits_{[n]\in\Delta}\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}((B_{0}^{n/A_{0}})^{+}/\pi)

is an equivalence (here
(B_{0}^{n/A_{0}})^{+}
denotes the
+
-ring for the
n
-th stage of the Čech nerve of
Y_{0}\to X_{0}
). Using the arguments from
Lemma

2.40
, we see that this implies that the functor

\D^{a}_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}/\pi)\to\varprojlim\limits_{[n]\in\Delta}\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}((B^{n/A})^{+}/\pi)

is an equivalence as well. More precisely, the critical statement to check is that for any morphism
g\colon X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\to X_{0}=\Spa(A_{0},A_{0}^{+})
of affinoid perfectoid spaces over
\Spa(\mathbb{F}_{p}((\pi^{1/p^{\infty}})))
the pushforward
g_{\ast}^{a}\colon\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)\to\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}_{0}/\pi)
on almost categories is conservative. This however follows formally from conservativity of
g_{\ast}\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}_{0}/\pi)
by embedding the almost categories via
\ast
-pushforward, i.e., the functors of almost elements over
A^{+}/\pi
resp.
A^{+}_{0}/\pi
, into
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)
resp.
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}_{0}/\pi)
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

The same argument as in
Theorem

4.7
can be used to show the following: Let
X\to S
be a map in
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
. Then there is a unique hypercomplete v-sheaf

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Perfd}_{/S})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty},\qquad T\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X_{T}})

such that if
T
admits a quasi-pro-étale map to some totally disconnected space then
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X_{T}})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+})
, where
X\times_{S}T=\Spa(B,B^{+})
. Indeed,
Proposition

4.9
implies descent after base change.

We note that logically we did not use
Theorem

1.2
in the proof of
Theorem

4.7
, only in its disguise through the key ingredient
Lemma

4.8
and the étale descent from
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.22]
.

Remark 4.12
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
with pseudo-uniformizer
\pi\in A
. It follows from
Theorem

4.7
and
Lemma

2.10
that

\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{+}/\pi}(\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}))\cong\D^{a}_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X}/\pi),

with the right hand side defined in
[
17
, Definition 3.1.3]
. Indeed, both sides satisfy
v
-descent and if
X
is totally disconnected we can apply
Lemma

2.10
.

Even though the present paper focuses on understanding the category
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
for (affinoid) perfectoid spaces, it is sometimes useful to extend the definition to general small v-stacks. Let us therefore introduce the following notation:

Definition 4.13
.

(a)

We denote by

\displaystyle\vStacks^{\sharp}:=\vStacks_{/\Spd({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}

the category of
untilted small v-stacks
, i.e. small v-stacks
X
(in the sense of
[
23
, Definition 12.4]
) together with a map
X\to\Spd({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Note that if
X
is representable, i.e. given by a perfectoid space in characteristic
p
, then a map
X\to\Spd({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is exactly given by the choice of an untilt of
X
. In particular we can identify
\vStacks^{\sharp}
with the category of small v-stacks on
\mathrm{Perfd}
.

(b)

We denote by

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{(-)})\colon(\vStacks^{\sharp})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{C}at,\qquad X\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})

the unique hypercomplete v-sheaf of categories that restricts to the one from
Theorem

4.7
on perfectoid spaces.

(c)

For every map
f\colon Y\to X
in
\vStacks^{\sharp}
we denote

\displaystyle f^{\ast}\colon\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}^{a}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})\rightleftarrows\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y})\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0muf_{\ast}

the adjunction of pullback and pushforward functors, where
f^{*}
is defined as the restriction map of the sheaf
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{(-)})
.
17
17

17

It follows by
[
14
, Proposition 5.5.3.13]
that
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Z})
is presentable for any
Z\in\mathrm{Perfd}
. By reduction to the totally disconnected case it follows that
f^{\ast}
preserves colimits, and hence admits a right adjoint.

(d)

If
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
is an affinoid perfectoid space, we denote by

\displaystyle\widetilde{(-)}\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})

the natural functor, and by
\Gamma(X,-)
its right adjoint.

The major goal of this paper is to find a large class of affinoid perfectoid spaces
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
for which we have
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
. The next two results provide useful abstract criteria for when this identification is valid. The first result allows one to argue via descent of modules from a totally disconnected covers, while the second result allows a reduction of the descent modulo
\pi
if one has a good enought understanding of
\pi
-complete objects.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
. Assume that there exists a quasi-pro-étale cover
Y\to X
with
Y=\Spa(B,B^{+})
totally disconnected such that almost modules (in the sense of
Definition

4.2
) descent along
(A^{+})_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}\to(B^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
. Then the natural functor

\widetilde{(-)}\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let
Y_{\bullet}\to X
be the Čech nerve of
Y\to X
and write
Y_{n}=\Spa(B_{n},B_{n}^{+})
. Note that each
Y_{n},n\geq 0
, is quasi-pro-étale over the totally disconnected space
Y=Y_{0}
. Then

\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})\cong\varprojlim\limits_{[n]\in\Delta}\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y_{n}})\cong\varprojlim\limits_{[n]\in\Delta}\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B_{n}^{+})\cong\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}),

using
Theorem

4.7
in the second isomorphism and that almost modules descent along
(A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(B^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
in the third.
∎

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
with pseudouniformizer
\pi\in A
. Then the functor

\widetilde{(-)}\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
is an equivalence if and only if the following conditions are satisfied:

1.

The functor
\widetilde{(-)}\colon\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X}/\pi)
is an equivalence.

2.

\Gamma(X,-)\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
preserves colimits.

3.

\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
is generated under colimits by
\pi
-complete objects
\mathcal{N}
with
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{N})
bounded to the right.

Proof.

The necessity is clear (as
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
is generated by
\pi
-complete objects), so let us check the converse. We need to show that the adjoint functors

\displaystyle\widetilde{(-)}\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\rightleftarrows\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 2.0mu\Gamma(X,-)

are inverse to each other. We first show that
\widetilde{(-)}
is fully faithful, i.e. that for every
M\in\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
we have
\Gamma(X,\widetilde{M})=M
. Since
\Gamma(X,-)
preserves colimits, we can assume that
M=P^{a}
for a compact generator
P\in\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
, in particular
M
is
\pi
-complete and bounded to the right. We claim that
\widetilde{M}\in\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
is
\pi
-complete. This can be checked on a
v
-cover
Y\to X
with
Y
totally disconnected. By naturality of
\widetilde{(-)}
and
Lemma

2.12
, this reduces to the case that
Y=X
, where
\widetilde{(-)}
is an equivalence. Thus
\Gamma(X,\widetilde{M})
and
M
are
\pi
-complete and hence the identity
\Gamma(X,\widetilde{M})=M
can be checked modulo
\pi
, where it is true by (a).

It remains to show that
\widetilde{(-)}
is essentially surjective. Since by assumption
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
is generated under colimits by
\pi
-complete objects, it is enough to show that every
\pi
-complete object lies in the essential image of
\widetilde{(-)}
. Given a
\pi
-complete
\mathcal{N}\in\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
we need to see that the counit
\widetilde{\Gamma(X,\mathcal{N})}\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\mathcal{N}
is an isomorphism. By assumption we may even assume that
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{N})
is bounded to the right. Then by the same argument as above both sides of the claimed isomorphism are
\pi
-complete, so we can check the claimed isomorphism modulo
\pi
. Then it reduces again to (a).
∎

4.3
Families of Riemann–Zariski spaces

In this subsection we provide some general results on families of Riemann–Zariski spaces which will enter in the proof of the descent on relative compactifications of totally disconnceted spaces in the next subsection (see
Proposition

4.21
below). Unless stated otherwise, each Huber pair is classical in this section. A related discussion can be found in
[
27
]
, but we need to ensure that the relative Riemann–Zariski space is a cofiltered inverse limits of
projective
schemes (and not merely of proper schemes) in order to ensure the uniform bound on descendability in
Lemma

4.17
.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
A^{+}\to A
be a map of classical, discrete rings such that each connected component of
X:=\Spa(A,A^{+})
is of the form
\Spa(K,A_{x}^{+})
for some field
K
and some subring
A_{x}^{+}\subseteq K
. Let
I
be the category of factorizations
\Spec A\to Y_{i}\to\Spec A^{+}
such that
Y_{i}
is a reduced projective
A^{+}
-scheme and the map
\Spec A\to Y_{i}
is dominant. Then:

(i)

I
is cofiltered and for every
i\in I
the map
\Spec A\to Y_{i}
factors uniquely over a map
X\to Y_{i}
over
\Spec(A^{+})
. The induced map
\lvert X\rvert\to\lvert Y_{i}\rvert
is closed.

(ii)

The map
\lvert X\rvert\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\varprojlim_{i}\lvert Y_{i}\rvert
is a homeomorphism of topological spaces.

We note that under the assumptions of this lemma the natural map
\Spa(A,A)\to\Spec(A)
is an isomorphism of locally ringed spaces, which makes condition (i) well-defined.

Proof.

Let us first show that
I
is cofiltered. Given any
i,i^{\prime}\in I
there can be at most one map
Y_{i}\to Y_{i^{\prime}}
in
I
. Indeed, this follows immediately from the fact that the map
\Spec A\to Y_{i}
is an epimorphism (in general dominant morphisms are epimorphisms in the category of reduced separated schemes, as one easily checks by a standard diagonal argument). Thus to prove that
I
is cofiltered we only need to check that there is some
i^{\prime\prime}\in I
such that there are maps
Y_{i^{\prime\prime}}\to Y_{i}
and
Y_{i^{\prime\prime}}\to Y_{i^{\prime}}
. But note that we can take
Y_{i^{\prime\prime}}
to be the scheme-theoretic image of the natural map
\Spec A\to Y_{i}\times_{\Spec A^{+}}Y_{i^{\prime}}
.

Now fix some
i\in I
. Note that
X
is the relative compactification of the map
\Spec A\to\Spec A^{+}
(see
[
17
, Example 2.9.27]
), so since
Y_{i}\to\Spec A^{+}
is proper (and thus its own relative compactification) we deduce that the map
\Spec A\to Y_{i}
extends canonically to a map
X\to Y_{i}
over
\Spec(A^{+})
. The uniqueness of the extension can be checked on connected components, where it follows from the valuative criterion for properness. We now check that the morphism
X\to Y_{i}
is closed. As
A
is discrete, the morphism
X\to Y_{i}
is a quasi-compact morphism of spectral spaces. Because it is as well specializing (by the valuative criterion for properness) this implies that the morphism
X\to Y_{i}
is closed. Indeed, by quasi-compactness of
X\to Y_{i}
the image of a closed subsets is pro-constructible and stable under specialization, hence closed (
[
28
, Lemma 0903]
). This proves (i).

It remains to prove (ii). By (i) we know that the map
\lvert X\rvert\to\varprojlim_{i}\lvert Y_{i}\rvert
is closed and continuous, so it is enough to show that this map is bijective. Let us first check that it is injective, so let
y,y^{\prime}\in\lvert X\rvert
be two given points which get mapped to the same point in each
\lvert Y_{i}\rvert
. Let
\widetilde{A^{+}}
be the integral closure of the image of
A^{+}
in
A
. Then
\pi_{0}(\Spec(\widetilde{A^{+}}))=\pi_{0}(\Spec(A))
and writing
\widetilde{A^{+}}
as a filtered colimit of finite, reduced
A^{+}
-algebras, we see that
y
and
y^{\prime}
lie in the same connected component
x=\Spa(K,A^{+}_{x})
of
X
and hence correspond to valuation rings
V_{y},V_{y^{\prime}}\subseteq K
containing
A^{+}_{x}
. Assuming
y\neq y^{\prime}
we can w.l.o.g. assume that there is some
\overline{a}\in V_{y}\setminus V_{y^{\prime}}
. Let
a\in A
be a lift of
\overline{a}
, which we can assume to be a unit (note that
K
is the filtered colimit of the ring of functions of clopen neighbourhoods of
\Spec K\subseteq\Spec A
). Then the pair
\{1,a\}
determines a map
f\colon\Spec A\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{A^{+}}
and we let
Y_{a}
be the scheme theoretic image of
f
. Then
Y_{a}\in I
and we claim that the induced map
X\to Y_{a}
separates
y
and
y^{\prime}
. To see this, we can assume that the map
A^{+}\to A
is injective, because otherwise the surjection from
A^{+}
to its image in
A
induces a closed immersion on schemes and in particular an injective map. Now
Y_{a}
can be described explicitly: It is obtained by gluing
\Spec A^{+}[a]
and
\Spec A^{+}[1/a]
along
\Spec A^{+}[a,1/a]
. Moreover, the map
X\to Y_{a}
sends a valuation ring
V\subseteq K
to the unique point on
Y_{a}
such that
V
dominates the associated local ring. Thus
V_{y^{\prime}}
is sent to a point in
\Spec A^{+}[1/a]
which does not lie in
\Spec A^{+}[a,1/a]
, whereas
V_{y}
is sent to a point in
\Spec A^{+}[a]
.

We now prove surjectivity of the map
\lvert X\rvert\to\varprojlim_{i}\lvert Y_{i}\rvert
, so let any
(y_{i})_{i}\in\varprojlim_{i}\lvert Y_{i}\rvert
be given. Replacing
A^{+}
by
\widetilde{A^{+}}
we may assume that
\pi_{0}(\Spec(A))=\pi_{0}(\Spec(A^{+}))
. Then all
y_{i}
live over the same connected component of
X
, so by base-changing to this connected component we can assume that
X
is connected, i.e. of the form
X=\Spa(K,A^{+})
for a field
K
. For each
i
the dominant map
\Spec K\to Y_{i}
induces an injection
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{i},y_{i}}\subseteq K
. Let
R\subseteq K
be the (filtered) union of all
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{i},y_{i}}
. Then
R
is a local ring and hence dominated by some valuation ring
V\subseteq K
. This valuation ring corresponds to a point
y\in\lvert X\rvert
which maps to
(y_{i})_{i}
(by uniqueness), as desired.
∎

The next lemma yields the crucial uniform descendability bound for
Proposition

4.21
. It is the analog of
[
17
, Proposition 2.10.10]
for families of Riemann–Zariski spaces.

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
A^{+}\to A
be a map of classical, discrete rings and
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
the associated discrete adic space. Assume that:

1.

Every connected component of
\Spec A
is of the form
\Spec K^{\prime}
for some field
K^{\prime}
and every connected component of
\Spec A^{+}
is of the form
\Spec V
for some valuation ring
V
.

2.

There is an integer
d\geq 0
such that for every map of connected components
\Spec K^{\prime}\to\Spec V
induced by
A^{+}\to A
, the associated map
V\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}K^{\prime}
is injective and the transcendence degree of
K^{\prime}
over the fraction field of
V
is
\leq d
.

Let
U=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{n}U_{j}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}X
be an open covering by quasicompact open subsets
U_{j}\subseteq X
. Then
U\to X
is descendable of index bounded by a constant only depending on
d
.

Proof.

Let
I
be as in
Lemma

4.16
. Then by
Lemma

4.16
we have
\lvert X\rvert=\varprojlim_{i}\lvert Y_{i}\rvert
and all the maps
\lvert X\rvert\to\lvert Y_{i}\rvert
are closed, which together implies that there is some
i\in I
and an open covering
Y_{i}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}W_{ij}
by qcqs open subsets
W_{ij}\subseteq Y_{i}
such that
U\to X
is the base-change of the map
W_{i}:=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{m}W_{ij}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}Y_{i}
along the map
X\to Y_{i}
. By
[
17
, Lemma 2.6.9]
it is thus enough to show that the map
W_{i}\to Y_{i}
is descendable of bounded index.

To simplify notation let us write
Y:=Y_{i}
and
W:=W_{i}
. By
[
17
, Corollary 2.10.7]
it is enough to show that
Y
can be covered by
d+1
affine open subschemes. The map
\pi_{0}(\Spec A)\to\pi_{0}(\Spec A^{+})
is a map of compact Hausdorff spaces and in particular has closed image. This image thus corresponds to an affine subscheme
\Spec A^{\prime+}\subseteq\Spec A^{+}
(the limit over all clopen neighbourhoods of the set-theoretic image inside
\Spec A^{+}
) by definition of
I
we know that
Y
is supported over
\Spec A^{\prime+}
. We can thus replace
A^{+}
by
A^{\prime+}
in order to assume that the map
\pi_{0}(\Spec A)\to\pi_{0}(\Spec A^{+})
is surjective. Now pick a connected component
x=\Spec V\in\pi_{0}(\Spec A^{+})
and let
Y_{x}
be the fiber of
Y
over
x
. Denote by
\eta
and
s
the open and closed point of
V
and let
Y_{x,\eta}
and
Y_{x,s}
be the the generic and special fiber of
Y_{x}
, respectively. We claim:

(
*
)

Y_{x,s}
has dimension
\leq d
.

To see this, we first claim that there are finitely many connected components
y_{1}=\Spec K_{1}^{\prime}
, …,
y_{n}=\Spec K_{n}^{\prime}
over
x
such that the map
y_{1}\mathbin{\sqcup}\dots\mathbin{\sqcup}y_{n}\to Y_{x}
is dominant. To see this, fix any irreducible component
Z\subseteq Y_{x,\eta}
. For every (qcqs) open subset
W\subseteq Z
, let
S_{W}\subseteq\Spec A
be the preimage of
W
under the map
\Spec A\to Y
. Then
S_{W}
is a quasicompact open subset and since the topological space of
\Spec A
is profinite it follows that
S_{W}
is closed. We claim that the intersection of all
S_{W}
, for
W
ranging through non-empty subsets of
Z
, is non-empty. Otherwise, by compactness there would be some
W_{1},\dots,W_{m}\subseteq Z
such that
S_{W_{1}}\mathbin{\cap}\dots\mathbin{\cap}S_{W_{m}}=\varnothing
. Since
Z
is irreducible,
W:=W_{1}\mathbin{\cap}\dots\mathbin{\cap}W_{m}
is still a non-empty open subset of
Z
and we deduce
S_{W}=\varnothing
; but this contradicts the fact that
\Spec A\to Y
is dominant. Pick any
y
in the intersection of all
S_{W}
; then the map
y\to Z
is dominant. Repeating this procedure for all irreducible components of
Y_{x,\eta}
, we find
y_{1},\dots,y_{n}\subseteq\Spec A
such that the map
y_{*}:=y_{1}\mathbin{\sqcup}\dots\mathbin{\sqcup}y_{n}\to Y_{x,\eta}
is dominant. By the assumption that the induced maps
V\mathrel{\hookrightarrow}K^{\prime}
are injective (see (b)) we see that every point of
Y_{x}
that is hit by some
y\in\Spec A
necessarily lies in
Y_{x,\eta}
. Consequently, since the map
\Spec A\to Y
is dominant, we deduce that
y_{*}\to Y_{x}
must be dominant.

With
y_{1},\dots,y_{n}
as in the previous paragraph, let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{n}\subseteq Y_{x}
be the scheme-theoretic images of these points under the map
\Spec A\to Y
. Then
Y_{x}=Z_{1}\mathbin{\cup}\dots\mathbin{\cup}Z_{n}
, so to prove claim (
*
) it is enough to show that each
Z_{i,s}
has dimension
\leq d
. Since
Z_{i}
is integral, by
[
28
, Lemma 0B2J]
it is enough to show that the generic fiber
Z_{i,\eta}
has dimension
\leq d
. But this follows immediately from the fact that
Z_{i}
is a projective
K
-variety and there is a dominant map
\Spec K^{\prime}_{i}\to Z_{i,\eta}
such that
K^{\prime}_{i}
has transcendence degree
\leq d
over
K
. This finishes the proof of claim (
*
).

With claim (
*
) proved, we now proceed with the proof that
Y
is covered by
d+1
affine open subschemes. Let
\kappa=\kappa(s)
be the residue field of
s
. By the proof of
[
17
, Lemma 2.10.9]
we can find some
n\geq 0
such that
Y\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{n}_{A^{+}}
and homogeneous polynomials
\overline{f}_{0},\dots,\overline{f}_{d}\in\kappa[t_{1},\dots,t_{n}]
such that the intersection of the vanishing loci of the
\overline{f}_{j}
’s in
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{\kappa}
is disjoint from
Y_{x,s}
. We can assume that each
\overline{f}_{j}
has a coefficient
1
and we can then lift each
\overline{f}_{j}
to a homogeneous polynomial
f_{j}\in A^{+}[t_{1},\dots,t_{n}]
, each of which has one coefficient
1
(first lift the coefficients to
V
and then use that
V
is the colimit of the functions on clopen neighbourhoods of
x\subseteq\Spec A^{+}
). Let
H_{j}\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{n}_{A^{+}}
be the zero locus of
f_{j}
. As in the proof of
[
17
, Lemma 2.10.9]
each
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{A^{+}}\setminus H_{j}
is affine. Now let
Y_{j}:=Y\mathbin{\cap}(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{A^{+}}\setminus H_{j})
. This is a closed subscheme of the affine scheme
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{A^{+}}\setminus H_{j}
and hence affine. Let
Y^{\prime}:=\bigcup_{j=0}^{d}Y_{j}
. This is an open subscheme of
Y
and by the argument in
[
17
, Lemma 2.10.9]
it contains
Y_{x}
.

Since the map
Y\to\Spec A^{+}
is closed, the image
Z^{\prime}\subseteq\Spec A^{+}
of
Y\setminus Y^{\prime}
under this map is closed. By construction we have
x\not\in Z^{\prime}
. By the argument in
[
23
, Lemma 7.5]
there exists a clopen neighborhood
U\subseteq\Spec A^{+}
of
x
contained in
\Spec(A^{+}\setminus Z^{\prime}
. Hence
Y_{U}=Y\times_{\Spec A^{+}}U
is covered by the affine opens
(Y_{0})_{U},\dots,(Y_{d})_{U}
.

The above proof shows that for every
x\in\pi_{0}(X)
there is a clopen neighbourhood
U_{x}\subseteq\Spec A^{+}
of
x
such that
Y_{U}
can be covered by
d+1
affine open subsets. By passing to a finite disjoint cover of
\Spec A^{+}
by
U_{x}
’s we can deduce that
Y
can be covered be
d+1
affine open subsets, as desired.
∎

In
Proposition

4.21
we used as well the following isomorphism of topological spaces (similar to
[
17
, Lemma 3.6.1]
). Note that for any Tate-Huber pair
(A,A^{+})
there exists a natural specialization map

\mathrm{sp}=\mathrm{sp}_{(A,A^{+})}\colon|\Spa(A,A^{+})|\to|\Spa(A^{\circ}/A^{\circ\circ},A^{+}/A^{\circ\circ})|,

which is uniquely determined by naturality and the requirement that if
(A,A^{+})=(K,K^{+})
for a non-archimedean valued field
K
and an open and bounded valuation subring
K^{+}\subseteq K
, then
\mathrm{sp}_{(K,K^{+})}
sends a point
x\in\Spa(K,K^{+})
corresponding to a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}_{x}
containing
K^{\circ\circ}
to the valuation on
\mathcal{O}_{K}/K^{\circ\circ}
corresponding to the prime ideal
