\mathfrak{p}_{x}/K^{\circ\circ}
.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
\Spa(A,A^{+})
be an affinoid Tate adic space such that every connected component is of the form
\Spa(K,A^{+}_{K})
, where
K
is a non-archimedean field and
A^{+}_{K}\subseteq K
is a subring. Then the specialiation map

\displaystyle\mathrm{sp}\colon|\Spa(A,A^{+})|\to|\Spa(A^{\circ}/A^{\circ\circ},A^{+}/A^{\circ\circ})|

is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

Without loss of generality
A^{+}\subseteq A
is an open and integrally closed subring in
A^{\circ}
. We denote
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
,
\overline{A}=A^{\circ}/A^{\circ\circ}
,
\overline{A}^{+}=A^{+}/A^{\circ\circ}
and
\overline{X}=\Spa(\overline{A},\overline{A}^{+})
. Then the claim is that the specialization map induces a homeomorphism
\lvert X\rvert\cong\lvert\overline{X}\rvert
.

First note that there is a canonical homeomorphism
\pi_{0}(X)=\pi_{0}(\overline{X})
, as both can be derived from the idempotent elements in
A^{+}
.
Fix a connected component
c=\Spa(K,A^{+}_{K})
of
X
and let
\overline{c}
be the corresponding connected component of
\overline{X}
. Let us write
\overline{c}=\Spa(\overline{K},\overline{A}^{+}_{K})
. Then
K^{\circ}
is the completed filtered colimit of
\mathcal{O}^{\circ}_{X}(W)
for clopen neighbourhoods
W\subseteq X
of
c
as the clopen neighborhoods of
c
are cofinal among all neighborhoods of
c
(by the argument in
[
23
, Lemma 7.5]
). Similarly,
\overline{K}
is the filtered colimit of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}(\overline{W})
for clopen neighbourhoods
\overline{W}\subseteq\overline{X}
of
\overline{c}
. By the homeomorphism of spaces of connected components, the
W
correspond to
\overline{W}
and under this correspondence we have
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}(\overline{W})=\mathcal{O}^{\circ}_{X}(W)/\mathcal{O}^{\circ\circ}_{X}(W)
. It follows that
\overline{K}=K^{\circ}/K^{\circ\circ}
, which is a discrete field.

With
c
and
\overline{c}
as above, we note that the points of
c
are precisely the open and bounded valuation rings
V
of
K
such that
A^{+}_{K}\subseteq V
. Similarly, the points of
\overline{c}
are the valuation rings
\overline{V}
of
\overline{K}
such that
\overline{A}^{+}_{K}\subseteq\overline{V}
. These two sets agree as each open and bounded valuation ring
V
of
K
must contain
K^{\circ\circ}
. As
c
was arbitrary (and
\pi_{0}(X)=\pi_{0}(\overline{X})
) this shows that the specialization map is bijective, and as well specializing. It remains to show that this bijection is a homeomorphism. For this it suffices to see that the specialization map is continuous and quasi-compact, e.g., using
[
23
, Lemma 2.5]
. Note that
\overline{X}
(and
X
) satisfy the assumption of
Lemma

4.19
. Hence the topology of
X
has a basis given by
U_{\overline{f},\overline{W}}=\{|\overline{f}|\leq 1\}\mathbin{\cap}W
for
f\in A^{\circ}
(with reduction
\overline{f}\in A^{\circ}/A^{\circ\circ}
) and clopen
\overline{W}\subseteq\overline{X}
. Now,
\mathrm{sp}^{-1}(\{|\overline{f}|\leq 1\})=\{|f|\leq 1\}
as can be checked by reducing to the case that
(A,A^{+})
is an affinoid field, and
\mathrm{sp}^{-1}(\overline{W})=W
where
W\subseteq X
is the clopen subset corresponding to
\overline{W}
under the homeomorphism
\pi_{0}(X)\cong\pi_{0}(\overline{X})
. In particular, we can conclude that
\mathrm{sp}^{-1}(U_{\overline{f},\overline{W}})
is open and quasi-compact. This finishes the proof.
∎

The proof of
Lemma

4.18
made use of the following slightly technical result about the topology on families of Zariski–Riemann spaces:

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be an affinoid adic space such that every connected component is of the form
\Spa(K,A^{+}_{K})
, where
K
is a field and
A^{+}_{K}\subseteq K
is a subring. Then
\lvert X\rvert
has a basis of open subsets given by
\{\lvert f\rvert\leq 1\}\mathbin{\cap}W
for varying
f\in A^{\circ}
and clopen subsets
W\subseteq X
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
X
is connected, so that
A
is a field. A basis of open subsets of
X
is given by the rational subsets, i.e. subsets of the form
\{\lvert f_{1}\rvert\leq\lvert g\rvert\neq 0,\dots,\lvert f_{n}\rvert\leq\lvert g\rvert\neq 0\}\subseteq X
for certain
f_{1},\dots,f_{n},g\in A
. We can assume that
g\neq 0
, so that
g
is invertible. Then the above subset agrees with
\{\lvert\frac{f_{1}}{g}\rvert\leq 1,\dots\lvert\frac{f_{n}}{g}\rvert\leq 1\}
and hence is an intersection of subsets of the form
\{\lvert f\rvert\leq 1\}
for varying
f\in A
. Note that if
f
is not in
A^{0}
(i.e. is not powerbounded) then
\{\lvert f\rvert\leq 1\}=\varnothing
. Indeed,
A
is either discrete or non-archimedean. If
A
is discrete, then
A=A^{\circ}
, and if
A
is non-archimedean, then
\{\lvert f\rvert\leq 1\}\neq\varnothing
implies that the point given by the non-trivial rank
1
valuation on
A
lies in
\{\lvert\leq\rvert 1\}
, which in turn yields
f\in A^{\circ}
. This finishes the proof in the case that
X
is connected.

Let now
X
be general. We argue as in the proof of
[
17
, Lemma 3.6.1]
. Fix some open subset
U\subseteq X
. Pick a point
x\in U
and let
c=\Spa(K,A^{+}_{K})\in\pi_{0}(X)
denote the connected component of
X
containing
x
. By what we have shown above, there is some
\overline{f}\in A^{+}_{K}
such that
x\in\{\lvert\overline{f}\rvert\leq 1\}\subseteq U\mathbin{\cap}c
. Note that
c
is the cofiltered limit of its clopen neighbourhoods in
X
, which implies that
A^{+}_{K}
is the completed filtered colimit of
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X}(V)
for the clopen neighbourhoods
W\subseteq X
of
x
. Therefore, after potentially modifying
\overline{f}
by some topologically nilpotent element in
A^{+}_{K}
(which does not affect
\{\lvert\overline{f}\rvert\leq 1\}
) we can extend
\overline{f}
to some
f_{W}\in\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X}(W)
for some
W
as before. Then

\displaystyle\bigcap_{c\subseteq W^{\prime}\subseteq W}(W^{\prime}\mathbin{\cap}X\setminus U\mathbin{\cap}\{\lvert f_{W}\rvert\leq 1\})=\varnothing,

where
W^{\prime}
ranges through all clopen neighbourhoods of
c
contained in
W
. Each term in the above intersection is closed in the construcible topology on
X
, so by compactness of the constructible topology, already a finite intersection must be empty. This implies that one of the terms must be empty, i.e. there is some clopen neighbourhood
c\subseteq W^{\prime}\subseteq W
such that
W^{\prime}\mathbin{\cap}X\setminus U\mathbin{\cap}\{\lvert f_{W}\rvert\leq 1\}=\varnothing
. Now let
f\in A^{+}
be the element which restricts to
f_{W}
on
W^{\prime}
and to
0
outside of
W^{\prime}
. Let furthermore
f^{\prime}\in A^{+}
be the elment which is
0
on
W^{\prime}
and
1
outside of
W^{\prime}
. Then
\{\lvert f\rvert\leq 1\}\mathbin{\cap}W^{\prime}\subseteq U
. Note that the left-hand side contains
x
by construction, so we have constructed an open neighbourhood of
x
inside
U
which belongs to the claimed basis for the topology. This finishes the proof.
∎

4.4
Descent for relative compactifications of totally disconnected spaces

In this section we want to identify
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
for certain relative compactifications of totally disconnected spaces. The precise statement is the following.

Theorem 4.20
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
whose tilt admits a map
f\colon X^{\flat}\to Z
to some totally disconnected space
Z
with
\mathrm{dim.trg}f<\infty
. Let
X^{\prime}\to X
be a quasi-pro-étale map, where
X^{\prime}
is a totally disconnected space. Then for
Y:=\overline{X}^{\prime/X}=\Spa(B,B^{+})
the natural functor

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Set
Z^{\prime}:=(X^{\prime})^{\flat}
. We let
X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
be the unique map of perfectoid spaces whose tilt is the map
Z^{\prime}\to\overline{Z^{\prime}}^{/Z}
(note that
\mathcal{O}(X^{\prime})=\mathcal{O}(Y^{\prime})
as this holds for the tilt). We note that there exists a natural inclusion
Y\to Y^{\prime}
whose tilt is the natural map
\overline{Z^{\prime}}^{/X}\to\overline{Z^{\prime}}^{/Z}
. In fact,
Y
is an intersection of rational open subsets in
Y^{\prime}
as this holds on the tilt. By
Proposition

4.21
there is a pro-étale cover
W^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
which is of universal almost
+
-descent, where
W^{\prime}
is a totally disconnected space, i.e., almost
+
-modules (in the sense of
Definition

4.2
) descend along
W^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
and all its base changes to affinoid perfectoid spaces. Then
W:=Y\times_{Y^{\prime}}W\to Y
is a pro-étale cover with
+
-descent for
Y
, and
W
is still totally disconnected (as it is an intersection of rational open subsets in the totally disconnected space
W^{\prime}
). By
Lemma

4.14
this finishes the proof.
∎

In the next proposition, an untilt of a perfectoid space
Z
of characteristic
p
is a perfectoid space
X
together with an identification
X^{\flat}\cong Z
.
Proposition

4.21
is similar to
[
17
, Proposition 3.1.11]
. Again the essential point is to show that open covers of relative compactifications are descendable
with uniformly bounded index
. This will as in
[
17
, Proposition 3.1.11]
be reduced to open covers of (families of) Riemann–Zariski spaces. The necessary results on these kind of adic spaces were discussed in
Section

4.3
.

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
f\colon Z^{\prime}\to Z
be a map of totally disconnected spaces in
\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
with
\mathrm{dim.trg}f<\infty
. Let
X
be an untilt of
\overline{Z}^{\prime/Z}
. Then there is a pro-étale cover
Y\to X
by a totally disconnected space
Y
such that almost
+
-modules descend along
Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
for any morphism
X^{\prime}\to X
of affinoid perfectoid spaces.

Proof.

Let
Y:=X^{\mathrm{wl}}
be the w-localization of
X
, which by
[
23
, Lemma 7.13]
is a totally disconnected space of the form
Y=\varprojlim_{i}Y_{i}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}X
such that each
Y_{i}
is a disjoint union of rational open subsets of
X
. We will show that the map
Y\to X
has the claimed descent properties and similarly any base change. Write
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
,
Y=\Spa(C,C^{+})
. It suffices to check that the map
(A^{\circ},A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(C^{\circ},C^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
is weakly adically descendable of an index
\leq c(d)
, where
c(d)
is a constant only depending on
d:=\mathrm{dim.trg}f
. Indeed, then almost
+
-modules will descent universally along
Y\to X
because
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{\circ},A^{+})
and
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
define the same almost category using
(A^{\circ})^{a}=(A^{+})^{a}
(and the similar statement for
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(C^{\circ},C^{+})
and
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(C^{+})
). By
2.33.(iii)
it is enough to show that for each map
Y_{i}=\Spa(C_{i},C_{i}^{+})\to X
the map
(A^{\circ},A^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}\to(C_{i}^{\circ},C_{i}^{+})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}
of adic analytic rings is adically descendable with an index
\leq c(d)
. By definition of adic descendability it is enough to prove this statement modulo
\pi
for every pseudouniformizer
\pi
on
X
. Now we can argue as in
[
17
, Proposition 3.1.11]
, using
Lemma

4.17
in place of
[
17
, Proposition 2.10.10]
in order to get descendability (and not just fs-descendability) of the covering. In the following we provide the details.

We are given a map
U:=Y_{i}=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{n}U_{j}\to X
for a cover of
X
by qcqs open subsets
U_{j}\subseteq X
. Write
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
,
Z=\Spa(B,B^{+})
and
Z^{\prime}=\Spa(B^{\prime},B^{\prime+})
, where we note that
B^{\prime}=A^{\flat}
and that
A^{\flat+}
is the completed integral closure of
B^{+}+B^{\prime\circ\circ}
in
B^{\prime+}
. Let
\overline{B}^{\prime}:=B^{\prime\circ}/B^{\prime\circ\circ}
,
\overline{B}^{+}:=B^{+}/B^{\circ\circ}
and
\overline{X}:=\Spa(\overline{B}^{\prime},\overline{B}^{+})
. Note that
\overline{B}^{\prime}=A^{\flat\circ}/A^{\flat\circ\circ}
, that
A^{\flat+}/A^{\flat\circ\circ}
is the integral closure of the image of
\overline{B}^{+}
in
\overline{B}^{\prime}
and that
X^{\flat}
satisfies the hypothesis of
Lemma

4.18
. Hence by
Lemma

4.18
we obtain a canonical homeomorphism
\lvert X\rvert=\lvert X^{\flat}\rvert\cong\lvert\overline{X}\rvert
. In particular, the open cover
X=\bigcup_{j}U_{j}
corresponds to an open cover
\overline{X}=\bigcup_{j}\overline{U}_{j}
and we define
\overline{U}:=\bigsqcup_{j}\overline{U}_{j}
.

We now claim that
\overline{X}
satisfies the condition of
Lemma

4.17
. Indeed, the connected components of
Z^{\prime}
are of the form
\Spa(K^{\prime},K^{\prime+})
for a perfectoid field
K^{\prime}
, hence the connected components of
\Spec\overline{B}^{\prime}
are of the form
\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{K^{\prime}})
(we note for later that this implies that
|\Spec(\overline{B}^{\prime})|
is a profinite set). Similarly, if a connected component of
Z
is of the form
\Spa(K,K^{+})
then the corresponding connected component of
\Spec\overline{B}^{+}
is of the form
K^{+}/\mathfrak{m}_{K}
, which is a valuation ring. This implies that the condition (a) of
Lemma

4.17
is satisfied. Similarly, condition (b) is satisfied using the same
d
(
[
5
, VI.10.3.Corollaire 1]
). Altogether we deduce that the map
\overline{U}\to\overline{X}
is descendable of index bounded by a constant only depending on
d
.
More precisely, by the proof of
Lemma

4.17
there is a reduced projective
\overline{B}^{+}
-scheme
\overline{S}
together with a dominant map
\Spec\overline{B}^{\prime}\to\overline{S}
such that the map
\overline{U}\to\overline{X}
comes via base-change from an open covering of
\overline{S}
along the map
\overline{X}\to\overline{S}
. Moreover,
\overline{S}
can be covered by
d+1
open affine subschemes
\overline{W}_{i}=\Spec\overline{R}_{i}\subseteq\overline{S}
. As
\overline{S}
is separated, the morphism
\Spec\overline{B}^{\prime}\to\overline{S}
is affine. Note that we have a surjective map
A^{\circ}/\pi\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}A^{\circ}/A^{\circ\circ}=A^{\flat\circ}/A^{\flat\circ\circ}=\overline{B}^{\prime}
with locally nilpotent kernel. By
[
28
, Lemma 07RT]
we can therefore form the pushout
S:=\Spec(A^{\circ}/\pi)\cup_{\Spec(\overline{B}^{\prime})}\overline{S}
in the category of schemes. By the construction in
[
28
, Lemma 07RT]
, the scheme
S
is covered by the
d+1
open affine subschemes
\Spec(A^{\prime}_{i})\cup_{\Spec(\overline{B}^{\prime}_{i})}\overline{W}_{i}
, where
\overline{Z}_{i}=\Spec(\overline{B}^{\prime}_{i})\subseteq\Spec(\overline{B}^{\prime})
is the affine open preimage of
W_{i}
along
\Spec(\overline{B}^{\prime})\to\overline{S}
, and
\Spec(A^{\prime}_{i})\subseteq\Spec(A^{\circ}/\pi)
is the unique affine open subscheme with underlying topological space
\overline{Z}_{i}
.

The morphism
\overline{X}\to\overline{S}
lifts to a morphism
X_{\pi}:=\Spa(A^{\circ}/\pi,A^{+}/\pi)\to S
which is the same map on underlying topological spaces. Now the given open covering
U=\Spa(C_{i},C_{i}^{+})\to X
induces an open covering
U_{\pi}:=\Spa(C_{i}^{\circ}/\pi,C_{i}^{+}/\pi)\to X_{\pi}
(using that
Lemma

4.18
applies to
U
as well), and
U_{\pi}\to X_{\pi}
reduces to the covering
\overline{U}\to\overline{X}
. Thus the covering
U_{\pi}\to X_{\pi}
is an open covering of discrete adic spaces which comes via base-change from an open covering of
S
(as can be checked after base change to
\overline{X}
). Since
S
is covered by
d+1
open affine subsets, the map
U_{\pi}\to X_{\pi}
is therefore descendable of index bounded by a constant only depending on
d
(
[
17
, Corollary 2.10.7]
). This finishes the proof.
∎

4.5
Cohomological boundedness conditions

In
Section

3.2
we introduced the notion of
p
-bounded morphisms of small v-stacks. In
[
17
, §3.5]
defines a related notion (also called
p
-bounded in loc. cit.) and proves strong descent results along such maps. In the present subsection we compare both notions of boundedness and in particular show that they are essentially equivalent up to the generality in which they are defined. By combining this observation with
Lemma

3.12
and
[
17
, Theorem 3.5.21]
, we get a powerful descent result for
\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)
on affinoid perfectoid spaces, which will enter the proof of our main result. In order to distinguish both boundedness conditions, let us rename the one from
[
17
, §3.5]
:

Definition 4.22
.

A map
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
of small
v
-stacks is called
+
-bounded if it is
p
-bounded in the sense of
[
17
, Definition 3.5.5.(b)]
.

Example 4.23
.

Let
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a morphism of affinoid perfectoid spaces in characteristic
p
. If
f
is quasi-pro-étale or weakly of perfectly finite type, then
f
is
+
-bounded (see
[
17
, Lemma 3.5.10.(v)]
and
[
17
, Lemma 3.5.13]
).

We now come directly to the promised comparison result of
p
-bounded and
+
-bounded morphisms:

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a morphism of small
v
-stacks which is locally separated and representable in prespatial diamonds. Then
f
is
+
-bounded if and only if it is
p
-bounded.

Proof.

Let us first assume that
f
is
+
-bounded. We verify that
f
satisfies the conditions of
p
-bounded maps in
Definition

3.7
. Condition (i) is true by assumption on
f
and condition (ii), i.e.
\mathrm{dim.trg}(f)<\infty
after pullback to every prespatial diamond, is part of the definition of
+
-bounded maps. It remains to prove condition (iii). By stability of
+
-bounded morphisms under base change (
[
17
, Lemma 3.5.10.(iii)]
) we may assume that
X
is a strictly totally disconnected space and by
Proposition

3.11
it is enough to show that
Y
is
p
-bounded. Since
f
is locally separated and
Y
is qcqs, we can reduce to the case that
f
is separated. Using
Lemma

3.6
,
[
11
, Proposition 3.6]
and
[
17
, Lemma 3.5.10.(iv)]
we can further pass to the relative compactification of
Y
over
X
in order to assume that
f
is proper.

Now let
y\in Y
be a maximal point, and
j\colon Y_{y}\to Y
the induced subdiamond. We need to show that
\mathrm{cd}_{p}(y)
is bounded by some constant
d
independent of
y
. For this it suffices to see that for every static étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
Y_{y}
the sheaf cohomology of
\mathcal{F}
is bounded to the right by
d
. Denoting
A:=j_{\ast}(\mathcal{F})
(which is static by
Lemma

3.6
), we can reformulate the problem to showing that
\Hom_{\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})}(\mathbb{F}_{p},A)\in\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})
is bounded to the right by
d
. But
A
is an overconvergent étale sheaf and by
[
17
, Theorem 3.9.23]
the category
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})^{\mathrm{oc}}
of overconvergent étale sheaves on
Y
embeds fully faithful into
\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi)^{\varphi}
of
\varphi
-modules in
\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi)
(here
\pi\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
is a pseudo-uniformizer). As this functor is given by tensoring with
\mathcal{O}^{+a}_{Y}/\pi
it preserves containment in
\D^{\leq 0}
. As
Y
is
+
-bounded and proper over
X
, we can conclude that
\Hom_{\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi)}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi,-)
has finite cohomological dimension by
[
17
, Lemmas 3.5.7.(i), 3.5.9]
. This implies the same claim for
\varphi
-modules, and hence the assertion.

We now prove the converse implication, so assume that
f
is
p
-bounded. In order to show that
f
is
+
-bounded, we can by
[
17
, Lemmas 3.5.10.(i),(ii)]
assume assume that
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
is a strictly totally disconnected space, which is furthermore of characteristic
p
. We may also reduce to the case that
Y
is separated (as
f
is locally separated and
+
-boundedness can be checked locally on the source by
[
17
, Lemma 3.5.10.(i)]
). By
Lemma

4.25
and
[
17
, Lemma 3.5.10.(iv)]
we can first replace
Y\to X
by its canonical compactification, and then reduce to the case that
Y
is spatial and separated.
We have to see now that for a pseudo-uniformizer
\pi\in A
, the pushforward

\displaystyle f_{\ast}\colon\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi,{\mathbb{Z}})\to\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi,{\mathbb{Z}})=\D^{a}(A^{+}/\pi)\otimes_{\D({\mathbb{Z}})}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}({\mathbb{Z}})

has finite cohomological dimension. Here,
\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi,{\mathbb{Z}})
refers to the category defined in
[
17
, Definition 3.5.2]
. In particular, the implicit
t
-structures are the natural one on
\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi,{\mathbb{Z}})
and the one on
\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi,{\mathbb{Z}})
defined by descent from the totally disconnected case, i.e.,
M\in\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi,{\mathbb{Z}})
lies in
\D_{\geq 0}
(resp.
\D_{\leq 0}
) if and only if this holds after pullback to any totally disconnected perfectoid space over
Y
. There is a natural equivalence

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y}/\pi,{\mathbb{Z}})\cong\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}:=\Mod_{\mathcal{O}^{+}/\pi}(\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})^{\mathrm{oc}}\otimes_{\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})),

generalizing
[
17
, Proposition 3.3.16]
to not necessarily discrete objects. Indeed, this follows by descent from the case of totally disconnected
Y
(see the proof of
Corollary

4.30
below for a very similar argument for
\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+})
in place of
\D^{a}_{{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}/\pi,{\mathbb{Z}})
and use that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})^{\mathrm{oc}}
as can be checked by descent from the strictly totally disconnected case).

Using transport of structure along the above equivalence, we obtain a
t
-structure on
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}
. We claim that this
t
-structure arises by restricting a
t
-structure on

\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}:=\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})^{\mathrm{oc}}\otimes_{\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)

to
\mathcal{O}^{+}/\pi
-module objects. Namely, if
Y
is strictly totally disconnected, then one checks easily that

\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}\cong\Mod_{C(\pi_{0}(Y),\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}A^{+a}/\pi}(\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi))

(see
4.27.(iii)
below for details in a slightly different version of that statement). Setting the
\D_{\geq 0}
-part (resp. the
\D_{\leq 0}
-part) to be the one of objects whose underlying object in
\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)
lies in
\D_{\geq 0}
(resp.
\D_{\leq 0}
) defines a
t
-structure on
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}
for which each pullback functor for a morphism
Y^{\prime}\to Y
of strictly totally disconnected spaces is
t
-exact (as
C(\pi_{0}(Y),\mathbb{F}_{p})\to C(\pi_{0}(Y^{\prime}),\mathbb{F}_{p})
is flat). If
Y
is not assumed to be a strictly totally disconnected space, we therefore obtain a
t
-structure on
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})
by descent, such that the pullback functors are
t
-exact. Clearly, this
t
-structure induces the given one for
\mathcal{O}^{+}/\pi
-module objects. Altogether we reduce the
+
-boundedness of
f
to showing that the functor

\widetilde{f}_{\ast}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}\to\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p}),

which is right adjoint to the natural symmetric monoidal functor

f^{\ast}\otimes\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi)\colon\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}},

has finite cohomological dimension for the
t
-structures on both sides. In fact, we may even restrict along
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})
, compatibly with the
t
-structure. Namely,
\widetilde{f}_{\ast}
is the base change along
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D^{a}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(A^{+}/\pi,\mathbb{F}_{p})
of the functor

\widetilde{f}_{\ast,\mathbb{F}_{p}}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}:=\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})^{\mathrm{oc}}\otimes_{\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}),

which is right adjoint to the natural symmetric monoidal functor
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}
. Moreover,
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}
acquires again a
t
-structure (by descent from the strictly totally disconnected case) such that it suffices to show that
\widetilde{f}_{\ast,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
has finite cohomological dimension for this
t
-structure.
It suffices to see that there exists some
d\geq 0
such that for any (
\kappa
-small) profinite set
S
the composition

\Gamma(S,-)\circ\widetilde{f}_{\ast,\mathbb{F}_{p}}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}\to\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})

has cohomological dimension bounded by
d
. Indeed, the functors
\Gamma(S,-)\cong\Hom({\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[S],-)
for
S
a profinite set are
t
-exact and a conservative family of functors on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
. The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(S,-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}_{\ast,\mathbb{F}_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G:=\mathrm{Id}_{\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})^{\mathrm{oc}}}\otimes\Gamma(S,-)}

commutes because the diagram of their left adjoints commutes (the left adjoints
(-)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[S]
respectively pullback along
f
act on different tensor factors). Note that we used that
\Gamma(S,-)
commutes with colimits and is
\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})
-linear to write the right adjoint of the pullback functor
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}
as
G=\mathrm{Id}_{\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})^{\mathrm{oc}}}\otimes\Gamma(S,-)
.

We claim that the functor
G
is
t
-exact for the given
t
-structure on the source and the natural one on the target. The functor
G
commutes with pullbacks in
Y
(as pullback along some
Y^{\prime}\to Y
and
\Gamma(S,-)
act on different tensor factors), which reduces the
t
-exactness claim to the case that
Y
is strictly totally disconnected. By construction,
t
-exactness can then be checked after applying the functor

\Gamma(Y,-)\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}\to\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}),

which commutes with
\Gamma(S,-)
and
G
. Indeed,
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,{\mathbb{F}_{p}}_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}})^{\mathrm{oc}}\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{C(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})}(\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}))
with
t
-structure induced by the one on
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, and similarly for
\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})
. Thus, the claimed exactness of
G
reduces to the
t
-exactness of
\Gamma(S,-)\colon\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})
, which is true.
Given the
t
-exactness of
G
we can finish the proof in the general case, i.e.,
Y
is not assumed to be strictly totally disconnected anymore. Then the pushforward
f_{\ast}\colon\D_{\mathrm{et}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})\to\D(\mathbb{F}_{p})
has cohomological dimension bounded by some
d\geq 0
because
Y
is
p
-bounded (by
Proposition

3.11
). Thus,
f_{\ast}\circ G\cong\Gamma(S,-)\circ\widetilde{f}_{\ast,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
has cohomological dimension bounded by
d
, which is independent of
S
, as desired.
∎

The previous proof used the following lemma to reduce the prespatial case to the spatial case. We extract it for reusability.

Lemma 4.25
.

Let
X
be a prespatial diamond, and let
X_{0}\subseteq X
be a spatial subdiamond with the property that
X_{0}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})=X(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
for all perfectoid fields
K
of characteristic
p
. Assume that
X\to Z
is a quasi-compact separated morphism to a small
v
-sheaf. Then the natural morphism
h\colon\overline{X_{0}}^{/Z}\to\overline{X}^{/Z}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

As
X\to Z
and
X_{0}\to X
are quasi-compact and separated, the morphism
h
is well-defined and proper (
[
23
, Proposition 18.7]
). In particular,
h
is qcqs and hence we may apply
[
23
, Lemma 12.5]
. Hence, it suffices to check that for any perfectoid field
\Spa(K,K^{+})
over
Z
, where
K^{+}\subseteq K
is an open and bounded valuation subring, the map
\overline{X_{0}}^{/Z}(K,K^{+})\to\overline{X}^{/Z}(K,K^{+})

is bijective. But by definition of canonical compactifications (
[
23
, Proposition 18.6]
), we get

\displaystyle\overline{X_{0}}^{/Z}(K,K^{+})

\displaystyle=X_{0}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})\times_{Z(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})}Z(K,K^{+})

\displaystyle\cong X(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})\times_{Z(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})}Z(K,K^{+})

\displaystyle=\overline{X}^{/Z}(K,K^{+})

by our assumption.
∎

4.6
Main theorem

In this section we will prove the remaining assertion of
Theorem

1.1
, i.e., that if
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
admits a map of finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
to a totally disconnected space, then

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})

is an equivalence. For this we will use the criterion of
Lemma

4.15
by using the (overconvergent) nuclear
\mathcal{C}
-valued sheaves from
Section

3.6
. The strategy can roughly be described as realizing the category
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
as a category of
\mathcal{O}^{+}
-modules in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(X,\Lambda)
, where
\Lambda={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]]
with
\pi
mapping to a pseudo-uniformizer in
A^{+}
.

In order to implement the strategy, we need to first introduce the following variant for nuclear
\mathcal{C}
-valued sheaves. In the following we recall the definition of
p
-bounded (pre)spatial diamonds from
Definition

3.4
.

Definition 4.26
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be an affinoid perfectoid space with pseudouniformizer
\pi
. Then for every
p
-bounded spatial diamond
Y
we denote

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}):=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])\otimes_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])}\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}),

with
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
as defined in
Definition

3.26
.

Note that the category
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
is not compactly generated, and hence not a nicely generated
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
-linear category in the sense of
Definition

3.38
. Nevertheless we get the following assertions analogous to
Proposition

3.41
:

Lemma 4.27
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be an affinoid perfectoid space with pseudouniformizer
\pi
. Then:

(i)

The assignment
Y\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
defines a hypercomplete sheaf of categories on the big quasi-pro-étale site of
p
-bounded spatial diamonds.

(ii)

For every
p
-bounded spatial diamond
Y
the category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
is generated under colimits by complete objects.

(iii)

If
Y
is a strictly totally disconnected space then
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(C(\pi_{0}(Y),A^{+}))
.

Proof.

Note that
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
is a nicely generated
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
-linear category. To prove this, let
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
be the family of compact generators
A^{+}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[S]
for profinite sets
S
, i.e.,
A^{+}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[S]=\alpha^{\ast}(A^{+}[S])
with
\alpha^{\ast}
as in
Lemma

2.5
. We show that they satisfy properties (i), (ii) and (iii) from
Definition

3.38
. Part (iii) is obvious by (
Eq.

3.38.1
). For (ii) we note that the functor
\Hom(A^{+}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[S],-)\colon\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
factors as the composition of functors

\displaystyle\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\IHom(A^{+}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[S],-)}}\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{\mathrm{forget}}}\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])\mathbin{\xrightarrow{(-)_{\mathrm{nuc}}}}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]]).

The first functor preserves colimits because compact objects in
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
are stable under tensor products. The second functor obviously preserves colimits, while
Proposition

2.19
shows that the third functor preserves colimits. This proves that property (ii) from
Definition

3.38
is satisfied. For property (i) we note that for every right bounded and complete
M\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
we have

\displaystyle M\otimes A^{+}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}[S]=(M\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]]_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]]_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}[S])\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]]_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}A^{+}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}},

so we conclude by
2.12.(iv)
. We have finally shown that
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
is a nicely generated
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
-linear category. Thus by
Proposition

3.41
all claims are true for
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,A^{+}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}):=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])\otimes_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])}\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
in place of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
. The almostification functor
(-)^{a}\colon\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
(with left adjoint
(-)_{!}
) realizes
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
as a retract of
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
, which shows that
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
is dualizable as a
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
-linear category (by
Remark

3.37
). This implies (i) by the argument in
Proposition

3.41
. Furthermore, part (ii) and (iii) follow from
Proposition

3.41
by
Lemma

2.35
.
∎

We note that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
is naturally a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category for a
p
-bounded spatial diamond. Indeed,
[
7
, Theorem 8.6]
implies that the category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
is stable under tensor products in
\D_{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])_{\omega_{1}}
.

Definition 4.28
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be an affinoid perfectoid space and
Y
a
p
-bounded spatial diamond over
X^{\flat}
. By
Lemma

4.27
and
Theorem

4.7
there is a unique algebra object
\mathcal{O}^{+a}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
such that for every strictly totally disconnected space
Z
over
Y
with untilt
Z^{\sharp}=\Spa(B,B^{+})
over
X
, the pullback of
\mathcal{O}^{+a}
to
Z
is the object

\displaystyle B^{+a}\in\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(C(\pi_{0}(Z),A^{+}))\cong\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Z,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})

with the last isomorphism supplied by
4.27.(iii)
.
We denote

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}):=\Mod_{\mathcal{O}^{+a}}(\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})).

The category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
is a precise version of the heuristic category of “
\mathcal{O}^{+}
-modules in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
”. The extra factor
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
accomodates the almost mathematics.

Lemma 4.29
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be a
p
-bounded affinoid perfectoid space with pseudouniformizer
\pi
. Then:

(i)

The assignment
Y\mapsto\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
defines a hypercomplete quasi-pro-étale sheaf of categories on the big quasi-pro-étale site of
p
-bounded spatial diamonds over
X^{\flat}
.

(ii)

For every
p
-bounded spatial diamond
Y
over
X^{\flat}
the category
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
is generated under colimits by complete objects.

(iii)

If
Y=\Spa(B,B^{+})
over
X
is strictly totally disconnected, then

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y^{\flat},(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+},A^{+}).

(iv)

For every separated
p
-bounded spatial diamond
Y
over
X^{\flat}
we have

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}},(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}).

Proof.

First note that the
p
-boundedness of
X
implies that every
Y\in X_{\mathrm{qproet}}^{\flat}
is
p
-bounded by
Lemma

3.6
.
Part (i) follows from
4.27.(i)
as in the proof of
Lemma

2.40
; we note that the pullback functors for
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
along quasi-pro-étale maps are the same as before on underlying objects in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
(because
\mathcal{O}^{+a}
is preserved under these pullbacks by definition). Part (ii) follows from
4.27.(ii)
by noting that
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
is generated under colimits by objects of the form
\mathcal{O}^{+a}\otimes\mathcal{M}
for
\mathcal{M}\in\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})
running through a set of generator. Part (iii) follows from
4.27.(iii)
and the definition of
\mathcal{O}^{+a}
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}_{B^{+a}}(\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(C(\pi_{0}(Y),A^{+})))\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{B^{+a}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{C(\pi_{0}(Y),A^{+})}(\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})))\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{B^{+a}}(\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}))

\displaystyle\qquad\cong\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+},A^{+})

using in the first isomorphism that
A^{+}\to C(\pi_{0}(Z),A^{+})
is integral mod
\pi
(and hence induces the analytic ring structure).
Part (iv) follows from
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
(and the overconvergence of
\mathcal{O}^{+a}
). Indeed, the claim can (by
Lemma

3.30
be checked in the case that
X
is strictly totally disconnected and
Y
qcqs. Then
\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}},Y
are strictly totally disconnected as they are quasi-pro-étale over
X
. Then the claim follows from
Lemma

3.29
as
\pi_{0}(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}})=\pi_{0}(Y)
.
∎

The next corollary is the culmination of all of our results on nuclear sheaves and the descent results of
\mathcal{O}^{+a}_{X}
-modules from
Section

4.4
. It lies at the heart of the proof of our main descent result. Note that in that corollary already the case
Y=X^{\flat}
is interesting and non-trivial. We refer the reader to
Definition

4.13
for the notation used in the corollary.

Corollary 4.30
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be an affinoid perfectoid space with pseudouniformizer
\pi
such that
X^{\flat}
admits a map of finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
to some totally disconnected space. Let
Y
be a separated
p
-bounded spatial diamond and let
f\colon Y\to X^{\flat}
be a morphism of finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
. Then there is a natural equivalence

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}})^{\sharp}})=\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y^{\sharp},(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}).

In particular, the following is true:

(i)

The category
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}})^{\sharp}})
is generated under colimits by right bounded, complete objects. The same holds true for
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
.

(ii)

The pushforward
\widetilde{f}_{\ast}\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}})^{\sharp}})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
preserves all small colimits.

Proof.

Note that the functor
Y\mapsto(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}})^{\sharp}
sends quasi-pro-étale covers to
v
-covers and preserves fiber products. Thus by
Theorem

4.7
the functor
Y\mapsto\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}})^{\sharp}})
satisfies quasi-pro-étale hyperdescent.
We first construct a natural map of hypercomplete quasi-pro-étale sheaves

\displaystyle\alpha(-)\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(-,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{(\overline{(-)}^{/X^{\flat}})^{\sharp}})

on the quasi-pro-étale site of
p
-bounded spatial diamonds over
X^{\flat}
. It is enough to construct this map on strictly totally disconnected spaces
Y
with untilt
Y^{\sharp}=\Spa(B,B^{+})
over
X
, where by
Theorem

4.7
and
4.29.(iii)
it boils down to the natural map
\alpha(Y)\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+},A^{+})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{(\overline{Y}^{/X^{\flat}})^{\sharp}})
(which is is compatible with pullback in
Y
).

In the following, we will identify quasi-pro-étale sheaves on perfectoid spaces over
X
with quasi-pro-étale sheaves on perfectoid spaces over
X^{\flat}
and view both sides as sheaves on perfectoid spaces over
X
. Now pick a quasi-pro-étale hypercover
Y^{\prime}_{\bullet}\to Y
by strictly totally disconnected spaces
Y^{\prime}_{n}
over
X
. Then each morphism
Y^{\prime}_{n}\to X
has finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
, and it suffices to see that
\alpha(Y^{\prime}_{n})
is an equivalence for any
n\in\Delta
. Thus, we may assume that
Y=\Spa(B,B^{+})
is strictly totally disconnected. Set
Z:=\Spa(B,A^{+})
. Then we may replace
X
by
Z
as this does not change both sides of
\alpha
(using
4.29.(iv)
) and
Z^{\flat}
still admits a morphism of finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
to a totally disconnected space. Hence, we may assume that
Y\to X
is quasi-pro-étale. We claim that
\alpha(Z)
is an equivalence. By
4.29.(iii)
and
4.29.(iv)
we have

\displaystyle\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Z,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})\cong\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})\cong\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+},A^{+}).

On the other hand, letting
C^{+}
be the minimal ring of integral elements in
B
containing the image of
A^{+}
, we obtain from
Theorem

4.20
that

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Z})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(C^{+})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+},A^{+}).

By going through the natural identifications one checks that
\alpha(Z)
is the obvious equivalence.

It remains to prove the “in particular” claims. Part (i) is an immediate consequence of
4.29.(ii)
(noting that the right boundedness holds for the constructed complete generators). The case
Y=X^{\flat}
implies the case of
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})
, where we note that
X^{\flat}
is
p
-bounded by
Lemma

3.12
. It remains to prove (ii). Under the equivalence
\alpha(Y)
the functor
\widetilde{f}^{\ast}
can be written as the composition

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}})\mathbin{\xrightarrow{-\otimes\mathcal{O}^{+a}}}\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,(\mathcal{O}^{+},A^{+})^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}),

where the first functor is induced (by tensoring over
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
with
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
) from the pullback
\rho^{*}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
for the morphism of sites
\rho\colon Y_{\mathrm{qproet}}\to\ast_{\mathrm{qproet}}.
Thus
\widetilde{f}_{\ast}
can be written as the composition of the right adjoints of the above two functors, so it is enough to show that each of these right adjoints preserves all small colimits. The right adjoint of
-\otimes\mathcal{O}^{+a}
is the forgetful functor, which clearly preserves all small colimits. The right adjoint
G
of the first functor can be obtained from the global sections functor
\rho_{*}\colon\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])\to\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
by tensoring with
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
. Indeed,
\rho_{\ast}
commutes with colimits and is
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
-linear because
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
is rigid, the left adjoint
\rho^{\ast}
is symmetric monoidal and the unit in
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
is compact (
Remark

3.37
,
Proposition

3.16
). This implies that
\rho_{*}\otimes_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])}\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
defines the right adjoint
G
of
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
by functoriality in the 2-category of
\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])
-linear presentable categories.
18
18

18

For this argument, we don’t need any theory of
(\infty,2)
-categories as the necessary natural transformation can be constructed directly by functoriality of
(-)\otimes_{\D_{\mathrm{nuc}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[\pi]])}\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
.
This proves the claim.
∎

We can finally show the main descent result of this paper, generalizing
[
17
, Theorem 3.5.21]
and finishing the proof of
Theorem

1.1
:

Theorem 4.31
.

Assume that
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})\in\mathrm{Perfd}^{\mathrm{aff}}
and that
X^{\flat}
admits a map with finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
to some totally disconnected space. Then

\displaystyle\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+}).

Proof.

We apply the criterion in
Lemma

4.15
. Condition (a) of that criterion follows from
[
17
, Theorem 3.5.21]
using
Propositions

4.24
and
3.12
. Conditions (b) and (c) follow from
Corollary

4.30
(and its proof to see the right boundedness of
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{N})
for the constructed complete generators in
Corollary

4.30
).
∎

We finish this section by providing another application of
Corollary

4.30
which will be important in
[
3
]
. In the following, recall the notation from
Definition

4.13
. We start with the following lemma:

Lemma 4.32
.

Let
f\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a morphism of untilted small
v
-stacks. Assume that
f
is qcqs, quasi-pro-étale and locally separated. Then
f_{\ast}\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y^{\prime}})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y})
commutes with colimits and base change in
Y
. If
f
is separated, then
f_{\ast}
is conservative.

Proof.

As
f
is qcqs, the commutation with colimits and the base change in
Y
are local on
Y^{\prime}
. In particular, we may assume that
f
is separated.
Moreover, we may by
v
-descent assume that
Y
is a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space (and we only have to check base changes to another strictly totally disconnected space). More precisely, compatibility with base change implies that conservativity can be checked
v
-locally. If now
Y=\Spa(A,A^{+})
is a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space, then
Y^{\prime}=\Spa(B,B^{+})
is as well by
[
23
, Proposition 9.6]
. But then,
f_{\ast}\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+})\to\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}^{a}(A^{+})
is just the forgetful functor. This implies commutation with colimits. Base change follows from steadiness of adic maps (see
Corollary

2.26
). Conservativity of
f_{\ast}
follows because by construction
f^{\ast}
sends a set of generators of
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
to generators of
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(B^{+})
, e.g., the almostifications of the compact objects in
\D_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})
.
∎

The promised application of
Corollary

4.30
is now the following. In that result, we say that an object
M\in\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y})
is right-bounded if this is true after pullback to any strictly totally disconnected space over
Y
.

Proposition 4.33
.

Let
X=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be an affinoid perfectoid space with pseudouniformizer
\pi
and assume that
X^{\flat}
admits a map of finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
to some totally disconnected space. Then for every separated
p
-bounded morphism of small v-stacks
f\colon Y\to X
the category
\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y})
is generated by right-bounded,
\pi
-complete objects, and the functor

\displaystyle f_{\ast}\colon\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y})\to\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X})=\D^{a}_{\hat{\scalebox{0.5}{$\square$}}}(A^{+})

commutes with colimits.

Proof.

Let
Z:=\overline{Y}^{/X}
be the canonical compactification of
f
. Then the inclusion
j\colon Y\to Z
is qcqs, quasi-pro-étale and separated. Thus,
j_{\ast}
is conservative and commutes with colimits by
Lemma

4.32
, hence
j^{\ast}
sends a collection of generators to a collection of generators. Now,
2.12.(iv)
implies that
j^{\ast}
preserves
\pi
-adic completedness of right-bounded objects. In particular, we may replace
Y
by
Z
(as
Z\to X
is again separated and
+
-bounded). Let
Z_{0}\subseteq Z
be a spatial subdiamond in the prespatial diamond
Z
, such that
Z_{0}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})\cong Z(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
for any perfectoid field
K
. Then by
Lemma

4.25

Z=\overline{Z_{0}}^{/X}
. By
Proposition

3.11
we can conclude that
Z
(and hence
Z_{0}
) is
p
-bounded, and then we can apply
Corollary

4.30
to
Z_{0}\to X
. This finishes the proof.
∎
